\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\widehat{C}}_{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{1-\theta^{2}}}+{\widehat{C}}_{5}\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\theta^{r}\left[\left(\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma^{2}(f_{j})}{V_{N}}}+\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}(h_{j})}\;\;\right)\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}(h_{j+r})}\;\;\right].

By assumptions II and III, there is a constant
{\widehat{C}}_{6}={\widehat{C}}_{6}({\overline{K}},m)
such that

\sum_{k}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{1,\xi}\dots\mathcal{L}_{k-1,\xi}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\xi}(1)\right\|_{1}\leq{\widehat{C}}_{6}\sqrt{{\varepsilon}}.

(16.18)

Claim 3 now follows from (
16.10
), (
16.14
), (
16.15
),
and (
16.18
).

Lemma
5.67
now follows from Claims 1–3 and (
16.7
).
\square

16.2
Proof of the LLT in the reducible case

Setup and reductions.
Let
\mathsf{f}=\{f_{n}\}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a Markov chain
\mathsf{X}=\{X_{n}\}
with state spaces
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
and marginals
\mu_{n}(E)={\mathbb{P}}(X_{n}\in E)
.
We assume that
\mathsf{f}
is not center-tight, and that
\mathsf{f}
is reducible.
In this case
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\delta(\mathsf{f})\mathbb{Z}
with some
\delta(\mathsf{f})>0
. Without loss of generality,

\delta(\mathsf{f})=1\ ,\ G_{ess}(\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}\ ,\ \mathbb{E}(f_{n}):=\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})]=0\text{ for all $n$,}

otherwise we center and rescale
\mathsf{f}
.

By the reduction lemma (Lemma
3.52
),
\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+\nabla\mathsf{a}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c}
, where

G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathbb{F})=G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathbb{F})=\mathbb{Z},

\mathsf{h}
has summable variances and
\mathbb{E}(h_{n}):=\mathbb{E}(h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1}))=0
,
\mathsf{c}=\{c_{n}\}
are constants, and
\mathbb{F},\mathsf{a},\mathsf{h},\mathsf{c}
are a.s. uniformly bounded.
There is no loss of generality in assuming that
\mathsf{a}\equiv 0
, because Theorem
15.1
holds for
\mathsf{f}
with
b_{N}
iff Theorem
15.1
holds for
\mathsf{f}-\nabla\mathsf{a}
with
b_{N}^{\prime}(X_{1},X_{N+1}):=b_{N}(X_{1},X_{N+1})+a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})-a_{1}(X_{1})
.

Henceforth we assume

\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c},

and
\mathbb{E}(f_{n})=\mathbb{E}(h_{n})=0
. So
c_{n}=-\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{F}_{n})
.
Let

c(N):=-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{F}_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1})].

(16.19)

By Theorem
5.4
, the following sum converges a.s.:

\mathfrak{H}(X_{1},X_{2},\ldots):=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1}).

Lemma 5.68

Under the previous assumptions,
for every
sequence of non-negative functions
v_{N+1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{N+1})
s.t.
\|v_{N+1}\|_{\infty}\neq 0

and for some
{\overline{\delta}}>0

\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{N+1}}v_{N+1}d\mu_{N+1}\geq{\overline{\delta}}||v_{N+1}||_{\infty},

(16.20)

for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,

\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})S_{N}}{v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})}\right)}{{{\mathbb{E}}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})})}=e^{2\pi imc(N)-s^{2}/2}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi mi\mathfrak{H}}\right)+o_{N\to\infty}(1).

(16.21)

where
o(\cdot)
term converges to 0 uniformly when
|m+is|
are bounded,
v_{N+1}
are bounded, and (
16.20
) holds.

Proof

Since the LHS of (
16.21
) remains unchanged upon multiplying

v_{N+1}
by a constant, we may assume that
\|v_{N+1}\|_{\infty}=1.

Fix
\epsilon>0
small and
r
so large that
\displaystyle\sum_{k=r}^{\infty}{\rm Var}(h_{k})<\epsilon
.
Fix
N
. Applying the Integer Reduction Lemma (Lemma
3.55
) to
\{\mathbb{F}_{n}\}_{n=r}^{N}
,
we obtain a decomposition

\mathbb{F}_{n}(x_{n},x_{n+1})=\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}^{(N)}(x_{n+1})-\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{(N)}(x_{n})+\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}+{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}(x_{n},x_{n+1})

where
\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}
are bounded integers, and
\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{(N)}(\cdot)
,
{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}(\cdot,\cdot)
are uniformly bounded measurable integer valued functions such that

\sum_{n=r}^{N}\|{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}\|_{2}^{2}=O\left(\sum_{n=r}^{N}u_{n}^{2}(\mathbb{F})\right).

There is no loss of generality in assuming that
\mathfrak{a}_{N+1}^{(N)}=\mathfrak{a}_{r}^{(N)}=0
, otherwise
replace
{\widetilde{f}}_{r}^{(N)}(x,y)
by
{\widetilde{f}}_{r}^{(N)}(x,y)-\mathfrak{a}_{r}^{(N)}(x)
, and
{\widetilde{f}}_{N}^{(N)}(x,y)
by
{\widetilde{f}}_{N}^{(N)}(x,y)+\mathfrak{a}_{N+1}^{(N)}(y)
.
Then
\displaystyle\sum_{n=r}^{N}\mathbb{F}_{n}=\sum_{n=r}^{N}(\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}+{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})
, whence

S_{N}-S_{r-1}=\sum_{n=r}^{N}f_{n}=\sum_{n=r}^{N}\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}+{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n}+c_{n}=\sum_{n=r}^{N}{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n}-\mathbb{E}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n}).

(16.22)

(The last equality is because
\mathbb{E}(S_{N}-S_{r-1})=0
.)

Let
\mathsf{g}
denote the array with rows
g^{(N)}_{n}:={\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n}-\mathbb{E}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n})

(n=r,\ldots,N)
,
N>r
. We claim that
\mathsf{g}
satisfies assumptions (I)–(III) of Lemma
5.67
.
(I) is clear, and (III) holds by choice of
r
and because
{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}
is integer valued. To see (II), note that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}(g_{n}^{(N)})=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)}+h_{n})=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})+\sigma^{2}(h_{n})+2{\rm Cov}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)},h_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})+\sigma^{2}(h_{n})+2\sigma({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})\sigma(h_{n})\leq 2\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})+\sigma^{2}(h_{n})\ \ (\because 2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2})

\displaystyle=O\biggl(\sum_{n=r}^{N}u_{n}^{2}(\mathbb{F})\biggr)+O(1),\text{ by choice of $\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}$ and $\mathsf{h}$}.

Since
\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c}
,
u_{n}^{2}(\mathbb{F})=u_{n}^{2}(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{h})\leq 2[u_{n}^{2}(\mathsf{f})+u_{n}^{2}(\mathsf{h})]
, see Lemma
1.15
(4). Thus by Theorem
2.30
and the assumption that
\mathsf{h}
has summable variances,

\sum_{n=r}^{N}u_{n}^{2}(\mathbb{F})\leq 2\sum_{n=r}^{N}u_{n}^{2}(f)+u_{n}^{2}(h)=O\bigl(V_{N}\bigr)+O(1)=O(V_{N}).

Assumption (II) is checked.

We now apply Lemma
5.67
to
\mathsf{g}
, and deduce that for every
{\overline{K}}>0
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
there are
{\overline{C}},{\overline{N}}>0
such that for all
N>{\overline{N}}+r
,
|s|\leq{\overline{K}}
, and
v_{N+1}
in the unit ball of
L^{\infty}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})(S_{N}-S_{r-1})}v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})\bigg|X_{r}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi imc^{(N)}}\cdot e^{-s^{2}/2}{{\mathbb{E}}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1}))}+\eta_{N-r}(X_{r}),

where
c^{(N)}:=-\sum_{n=r}^{N}\mathbb{E}({\widetilde{f}}^{(N)}_{n})
and
\|\eta_{N-r}\|_{1}\leq{\overline{C}}\sqrt{\epsilon}
. Since
\|v_{N+1}\|_{\infty}=1
, we also have the trivial bound
\|\eta_{N-r}\|_{\infty}\leq 2
.

We are ready to prove the lemma. The left-hand-side of (
16.21
) equals

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})S_{N}}v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})\right)}{{\mathbb{E}}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1}))}=

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})S_{r-1}}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{n}}})(S_{N}-S_{r-1})}v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})\big|X_{r}\right)}{{\mathbb{E}}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1}))}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[e^{i(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})S_{r-1}}\left(e^{2\pi imc^{(N)}-s^{2}/2}+\frac{\eta_{N-r}(X_{r})}{\mathbb{E}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1}))}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{A}{\underbrace{e^{2\pi imc^{(N)}-s^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}_{x}(e^{2\pi imS_{r-1}+o(1)})}}+O({\overline{\delta}}^{-1})\underset{B}{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{x}(\eta_{N-r}(X_{r}))}},\text{ as $N\to\infty$}.

We examine
A
,
B
. Let
\displaystyle{\widehat{c}}^{(r-1)}:=\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}c_{k}=-\mathbb{E}(\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}\mathbb{F}_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1}))
. Since
\displaystyle c(N)=-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{F}_{k})
,

\displaystyle c(N)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{F}_{k})-\sum_{k=r}^{N}(\mathbb{E}({\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})+\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)})\text{ because $\sum_{n=r}^{N}\mathbb{F}_{n}=\sum_{n=r}^{N}(\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}+{\widetilde{f}}_{n}^{(N)})$}

\displaystyle\equiv{\widehat{c}}^{(r-1)}+c^{(N)}\mod\mathbb{Z},\text{ because $\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{(N)}\in\mathbb{Z}$}.

By assumption,
\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c}
with
\mathbb{F}
integer valued. Necessarily,

\exp(2\pi imS_{r-1})=\exp(2\pi im\mathfrak{H}_{r}+2\pi im{\widehat{c}}^{(r-1)})

(16.23)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{r}:=\sum_{k=1}^{r-1}h_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1}).

By choice of
r
and Lemma
2.28
,

\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}_{x}(e^{i\xi\mathfrak{H}})-{\mathbb{E}}_{x}(e^{i\xi\mathfrak{H}_{r}})\bigr|\leq|\xi|{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\left|\mathfrak{H}-\mathfrak{H}_{r}\right|\right)\leq|\xi|{\rm Var}\biggl(\sum_{k=r}^{\infty}h_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1})\biggr)^{1/2}=O\left(\sqrt{{\varepsilon}}\right)

uniformly when
\xi
varies in a compact domain. Substituting (
16.23
) in
A
, we obtain

A=[1+o(1)]e^{2\pi imc(N)-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}}\right)+O\left(\sqrt{{\varepsilon}}\right).

Next, the exponential mixing of
\mathsf{X}
implies that for all
N
large enough,

B:=\mathbb{E}_{x}(\eta_{N-r}(X_{r}))=\mathbb{E}(\eta_{N-r}(X_{r}))+o(1)=O(\sqrt{\epsilon}).

Thus the left-hand-side of (
16.21
) equals

e^{2\pi imc(N)-s^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}_{x}(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}+o(1)})+O(\sqrt{\epsilon})
. The lemma follows, because
\epsilon
was arbitrary.

\square

Proof of Theorem
15.1
.

Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
, and assume
G_{ess}(\mathsf{f})=\delta(\mathsf{f})\mathbb{Z}
with
\delta(\mathsf{f})\neq 0
.

We begin with some reductions.
By Theorem
9.3
,
\mathsf{f}
has an optimal reduction, and we can write

\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+{\mathsf{F}}
where
\mathbb{F}
has algebraic range
\delta(\mathsf{f})\mathbb{Z}
and
{\mathsf{F}}

is a.s. uniformly bounded and center-tight. There is no loss of generality in assuming that

\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|{\mathsf{F}}|\leq\delta(\mathsf{f})
, since this can always be arranged
by replacing
{F}_{n}
by
{F}_{n}\mod\delta(\mathsf{f})
.
Next by the gradient lemma (Lemma
2.32
), we decompose

{\mathsf{F}}=\nabla\mathsf{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}+\widetilde{\mathsf{c}}

where
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{a}|\leq 2\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{F}|
,

\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}
has summable variances, and
\widetilde{c}_{n}
are constants.

It is convenient to introduce

\displaystyle f_{n}^{\ast}:=\frac{1}{\delta(\mathsf{f})}[f_{n}-\nabla a_{n}-\mathbb{E}(f_{n}-\nabla a_{n})].

G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f}^{\ast})=\mathbb{Z}
, and

\mathsf{f}^{\ast}=\frac{1}{\delta(\mathsf{f})}\mathbb{F}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c},

(16.24)

where
h_{n}:=\frac{1}{\delta(\mathsf{f})}[\widetilde{f}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{f}_{n})]
is a centered additive functional with summable variances,
and
c_{n}:=\frac{1}{\delta(\mathsf{f})}[\widetilde{c}_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{f}_{n})-\mathbb{E}(f_{n}-\nabla a_{n})]
.

We first prove the theorem in the special case when

\delta(\mathsf{f})=1
,
\mathbb{E}(f_{n})=0
for all
n
, and

\mathsf{a}\equiv 0
.

(16.25)

In this case
\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{f}^{\ast}
and (
16.24
)
places us in the
setup of Lemma
5.68
.
Given this lemma, the proof is very similar to the proof of the
local limit theorem in the irreducible non-lattice case, but we reproduce it for completeness. We focus on parts (2) and (3)
of the theorem, because part (1) follows from them.

Define as in (
16.19
),

\displaystyle c(N):=-\frac{1}{\delta(\mathsf{f})}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{F}_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1})]
, and
let

\mathfrak{H}:=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1}),\hskip 11.74988ptb_{N}:=\{c(N)\}.

Fix
\phi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that

{\rm supp}({\widehat{\phi}})\subset[-L,L]
,
and
let
v_{N+1}
denote the indicator function of

\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}
. By the Fourier inversion formula

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}(\phi(S_{N}-b_{N}-z_{N})|X_{N+1}{\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-L}^{L}{\widehat{\phi}}(\xi)\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{i\xi(S_{N}-b_{N}-z_{N})}v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})\right)}{{\mathbb{E}}(v_{N+1}(X_{N+1}))}d\xi

(16.26)

and the task is to find the asymptotic behavior of (
16.26
) in case
z_{N}\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\frac{z_{N}}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\to z
.

Let
K:=\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{f}|
and recall the constant
{\widetilde{\delta}}={\widetilde{\delta}}(K)
from Lemma
4.61
.
Split
[-L,L]
into a finite collection of subintervals
I_{j}
of length less than
\min\{{\widetilde{\delta}},\pi\}
,
in such a way that every
I_{j}
is either bounded away from
2\pi\mathbb{Z}
, or intersects it an unique point
2\pi m
exactly at its center.

If
I_{j}\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}=\varnothing
, then
\sum d_{n}^{2}(\xi)=\infty
uniformly on
I_{j}
(Theorem
9.5
). Thus by (
13.7
),

\Phi_{N}(x,\xi)\to 0
uniformly on
I_{j}
. In this case
we can argue as in the proof of (
13.18
)
and show that the contribution of
I_{j}
to the integral (
16.26
) is
o\bigl(V_{N}^{-1/2}\bigr).

If
I_{j}\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}\neq\varnothing
, then the center of
I_{j}
equals
2\pi m
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Fix some large
R
.
Let

J_{j,N}^{\prime}
be the contribution to the integral from the set
\{\xi\in I_{j}:|\xi-2\pi m|\leq RV_{N}^{-1/2}\}
,
and let

J_{j,N}^{\prime\prime}
be the integral over
\{\xi\in I_{j}:|\xi-2\pi m|>RV_{N}^{-1/2}\}
.

The main contribution comes from
J_{j,N}^{\prime}
, because
one can show as in Claim 2 in §
13.2
that

\displaystyle|J_{j,N}^{\prime\prime}|\leq C\int_{|u|>RV_{N}^{-1/2}}e^{-cV_{N}u^{2}}du\leq C\frac{e^{-cR^{2}}}{R\sqrt{V_{N}}}
, which is negligible for
R\gg 1
.

To estimate
J_{j,N}^{\prime}
,
we make the change of variables
\xi=2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}
. Since
z_{N}\in\mathbb{Z}
and

b_{N}=\{c(N)\}
, we have

\xi(S_{N}-b_{N}-z_{N})=\xi S_{N}-2\pi mc(N)-\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}(z_{N}+\{c(N)\})\hskip 11.74988pt\text{mod}\hskip 11.74988pt2\pi.

So

J_{j,N}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{V_{N}}}\left[\int_{|s|<R}{{\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m+\frac{s}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})}\frac{e^{-2\pi imc(N)}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{i\xi S_{N}}{v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})}\right)}{{\mathbb{E}}({v_{N+1}(X_{N+1})})}\;e^{-is\frac{z_{N}+O(1)}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}}ds\right].

Fixing
R
and letting
N\to\infty
, we see by Lemma
5.68
that

\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{j,N}^{\prime}=\frac{{\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m)}{2\pi}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im{\mathfrak{H}}}\right)\int_{|s|<R}e^{-isz-s^{2}/2}ds+o_{N\to\infty}(1)

=\frac{{\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m)}{\sqrt{2\pi}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}}\right)e^{-z^{2}/2}+o_{R\to\infty}(1)+o_{N\to\infty}(1).

Combining the estimates for
J_{j,N}
we obtain that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{j,N}=\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}}\right){\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m),

if
I_{j}
intersects
2\pi\mathbb{Z}
, and this limit is zero otherwise.
Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sqrt{V_{N}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}(\phi(S_{N}-b_{N}-z_{N})|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[-L,L]}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}}\right){\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m)=\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{H}}\right){\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{2\pi im\mathfrak{F}}\right){\widehat{\phi}}(2\pi m),{\text{ where $\mathfrak{F}\in[0,1)$,
$\mathfrak{F}:=\mathfrak{H}\mod\mathbb{Z}$}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\left(\mathcal{C}_{x}\phi\right)}(2\pi m),\text{ where }(\mathcal{C}_{x}\phi)(t):={\mathbb{E}}_{x}[\phi(t+\mathfrak{F})]

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{x}\phi\right)(m)\equiv\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[\phi(m+\mathfrak{F})],

by the Poisson summation formula.

This proves part (2) of the theorem in the special case (
16.25
), and in particular for the
additive functional
\mathsf{f}^{\ast}
defined above.
Now consider the general case:

S_{N}(\mathsf{f})-\mathbb{E}[S_{N}(\mathsf{f})]\equiv\delta(\mathsf{f})S_{N}(\mathsf{f}^{\ast})+a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})-a_{1}(X_{1})+\mathbb{E}[a_{1}(X_{1})-a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})].

Since part (2) of the theorem holds for
\mathsf{f}^{\ast}
with

\mathfrak{F}=\{\sum h_{n}\}\in[0,1)
and
b_{N}=\{c(N)\}
, it must hold for
\mathsf{f}
with
\delta(\mathsf{f})\mathfrak{F}
and

b_{N}(X_{1},X_{N+1}):=\delta(\mathsf{f})\{c(N)\}+a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})-a_{1}(X_{1})+\mathbb{E}[a_{1}(X_{1})-a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})].

Clearly
|b_{N}|\leq\delta(\mathsf{f})+4\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{a}|
. Recalling that
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{a}|\leq 2\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{F}|\leq 2\delta(\mathsf{f})
, we find that
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|b_{N}|\leq 9\delta(\mathsf{f})
, proving part (3) as well.

\Box

16.3
Necessity of the irreducibility assumption

Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
. Recall that
\mathsf{f}_{r}=\{f_{n}\}_{n\geq r}
and
\mathsf{X}_{r}=\{X_{n}\}_{n\geq r}
. In this section we prove Theorem
15.2
, which asserts the equivalence of the following three conditions:

(a)

\mathsf{f}
is irreducible with algebraic range
\mathbb{R}
.

(b)

(\mathsf{X}_{r},\mathsf{f}_{r})
satisfies the mixing non-lattice local limit theorem, for all
r
.

(c)

(\mathsf{X}_{r},\mathsf{f}_{r})
satisfies the mixing uniform distribution mod
t
for all
r
and
t>0
.

(a)
\Rightarrow
(b):
To see this recall that additive functionals on uniformly elliptic Markov chains are special cases of stably hereditary additive functionals on uniformly elliptic Markov arrays, and apply Theorem
12.3
(1) to
\phi
continuous with compact support which approximate indicators of intervals in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.

(b)
\Rightarrow
(a):
Assume
\mathsf{f}
satisfies the “mixing non-lattice LLT” property.
By definition,
V_{N}\to\infty
, and therefore
\mathsf{f}
is not center-tight.

Also,
G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
, otherwise
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})=0
for
z_{N}
and
(a,b)
such that
z_{N}+(a,b)\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
.

If
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
then
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible and we are done. Assume by way of contradiction that
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})\neq\mathbb{R}
, then
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=t\mathbb{Z}
for some
t>0
(
t=0
is impossible because
\mathsf{f}
is not center-tight). There is no loss of generality in assuming that

G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{and}\hskip 11.74988pt\mathbb{E}(f_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1}))=0\text{ for all $n$.}

Let
S_{N}^{(r)}:=f_{r}(X_{r},X_{r+1})+\cdots+f_{N}(X_{N},X_{N+1})
and
V_{N}^{(r)}:={\rm Var}(S_{N}^{(r)})
. By the exponential mixing of
\mathsf{X}
(Proposition
1.11
),

|V_{N}-V_{N}^{(r)}|=|V_{r-1}+2{\rm Cov}(S_{N}^{(r)},S_{r-1})|\leq V_{r}+2\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\sum_{k=r}^{\infty}{\rm Cov}(f_{j},f_{k})=O(1).

Therefore, for fixed
r
,
V_{N}/V_{N}^{(r)}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1
.

Since
G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{R})=\mathbb{R}
and
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathsf{f}
is reducible, and we can write

\mathsf{f}=\mathbb{F}+\nabla\mathsf{a}+\mathsf{h}+\mathsf{c},

where
\mathbb{F}
is irreducible with algebraic range
\mathbb{Z}
,
a_{n}(x)
are uniformly bounded (say by
K
),

\mathsf{h}
has summable variances,
\mathbb{E}(h_{n})=0
, and
\mathsf{c}
are constants.

Let

b_{N}^{(r)}(X_{r},X_{N+1}):=a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})-a_{r}(X_{r})+\left\{-\sum_{k=r}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{F}_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1}))\right\},

\mathfrak{F}:=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})\text{ , }\hskip 11.74988pt\mathfrak{F}_{r}:=\sum_{n=r}^{\infty}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1}).

By Theorem
5.4
, these sums converge almost surely and in
L^{2}
.

As
we saw in the proof of Theorem
15.1
, if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N}^{(r)})}{\sqrt{V_{N}^{(r)}}}\to 0
and
{\mathbb{P}}(X_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}_{n})
is bounded below, then for all
\phi\in C_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
x_{r}\in\mathfrak{S}_{r}
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}\mathbb{E}_{x_{r}}[\phi(S_{N}^{(r)}-b_{N}^{(r)}-z_{N})]=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[\phi(m+\mathfrak{F}_{r})].

(16.27)

We are going to choose
r,x_{r},z_{N},\mathfrak{A}_{N}
and
\phi
in such a way that (
16.27
) is inconsistent with (b). Here are the choices:

\circ

Choice of
r
: Since
\mathfrak{F}_{r}
is the tail of a convergent series,
\mathfrak{F}_{r}\xrightarrow[r\to\infty]{}0
a.s., whence in probability.
Choose
r
s.t.
{\mathbb{P}}(|\mathfrak{F}_{r}|\geq 0.2)<10^{-3}
.

\circ

Choice of
x_{r}
:
{\mathbb{P}}(|\mathfrak{F}_{r}|\geq 0.2)=\int{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(|\mathfrak{F}_{r}|\geq 0.2)]\mu_{r}(dx)
. So there exist
x_{r}\in\mathfrak{S}_{r}
s.t.

{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}(|\mathfrak{F}_{r}|>0.2)<10^{-3}.

\circ

Choice of
\mathfrak{A}_{N}
: By construction,
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|b_{N}^{(r)}|\leq 2K+1
. Divide
[-2K-1,2K+1]
into equal intervals of length less than
10^{-2}
. At least one such interval, call it
J_{N}
, satisfies

{\mathbb{P}}(b_{N}^{(r)}\in J_{N})\geq 10^{-2}(4K+2)^{-1}\text{ and }|J_{N}|\leq 10^{-2}.

Let

\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}^{(r)}:=[b_{N}^{(r)}\in J_{N}].

\circ

z_{N}:=-
center of
J_{N}
, then
z_{N}=O(1)
and
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N}^{(r)})}{\sqrt{V_{N}^{(r)}}}\to 0
.

\circ

Choose a sequence
N_{k}\to\infty
such that
z_{N_{k}}\to a
. Let
I:=-a+[0.4,0.6]
.

\circ

Choose
\phi\in C_{c}(\mathbb{R})
s.t.
0\leq\phi\leq 1
,
\phi|_{[0.3,0.7]}\equiv 1
and
\phi|_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[0.2,0.8]}\equiv 0
.

With these choices,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}^{(r)}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}\bigl(S_{N}^{(r)}-z_{N}\in I\big|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}^{(r)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N_{k}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}\bigl(S_{N_{k}}^{(r)}-z_{N_{k}}\in I\big|b_{N_{k}}^{(r)}(X_{r},X_{N_{k}+1})\in J_{N_{k}}\bigr)\ \left(\text{because }\frac{V_{N}^{(r)}}{V_{N}}\to 1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N_{k}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}\bigl(S_{N_{k}}^{(r)}-b_{N_{k}}^{(r)}-z_{N_{k}}\in[0.3,0.7]\,\big|\,b_{N_{k}}^{(r)}\in J_{N_{k}}\bigr),\text{(because for $k\gg 1$}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636ptI-b_{N_{k}}\subset I-J_{N_{k}}\subset I+\left(z_{N_{k}}-\tfrac{|J_{N_{k}}|}{2},z_{N_{k}}+\tfrac{|J_{N_{k}}|}{2}\right)\subset I+(a-0.1,a+0.1)\subset[0.3,0.7])

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N_{k}}}\mathbb{E}_{x_{r}}\bigl(\phi(S_{N_{k}}^{(r)}-b_{N_{k}}^{(r)}-z_{N_{k}})\,\big|\,b_{N_{k}}^{(r)}\in J_{N_{k}+1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{x_{r}}[\phi(m+\mathfrak{F}_{r})],\text{ by \eqref{hasata}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}\bigl(m+\mathfrak{F}_{r}\in[0.2,0.8]\bigr)\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{x_{r}}\bigl(|\mathfrak{F}_{r}|\geq 0.2\bigr)<10^{-3}<|I|.

But this contradicts (b).

(a)
\Rightarrow
(c):
Suppose
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
is non-lattice and irreducible, then

(\mathsf{X}_{r},\mathsf{f}_{r})
is non-lattice and irreducible for all
r
.
Fix
t>0
,
x_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
, and some sequence of measurable events
\mathfrak{A}_{n}\subset\mathfrak{S}_{n}
such that
{\mathbb{P}}(X_{n}\in\mathfrak{A}_{n})
is bounded below. Let
\displaystyle S_{N}^{(r)}:=\sum_{k=r}^{N}f_{k}(X_{k},X_{k+1})
.

We show that for every continuous and periodic
\phi(x)
with period
t
,

\mathbb{E}_{x}(\phi(S_{N}^{(r)})|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\phi(x)dx.

(16.28)

It is enough to show (
16.28
) for trigonometric polynomials

\displaystyle\phi(u)=\sum_{|n|<L}c_{n}e^{2\pi inu/t}
, as these are dense in
C[0,t]
. For such functions,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}(\phi(S_{N}^{(r)})|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})=\sum_{|n|<L}c_{n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(e^{2\pi inS_{N}^{(r)}/t}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})

\displaystyle=c_{0}+\sum_{0<|n|<L}\Phi_{N}\left(x,\tfrac{2\pi n}{t}|\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}\right),\text{where $\Phi_{N}$ are the characteristic functions of $(\mathsf{X}_{r},\mathsf{f}_{r})$}

\displaystyle=c_{0}+o(1),\text{ by irreducibility and \eqref{CondBoundChar}.}

Since
c_{0}=\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\phi(u)du
, (
16.28
) follows. Standard approximation arguments show that (
16.28
) implies that

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in(a,b)|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\frac{|a-b|}{t}\text{ for all intervals }(a,b).

(c)
\Rightarrow
(a):
We need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.69

Fix a regular sequence of sets
\mathfrak{A}_{N}
,
x
, and
t>0
, and suppose that

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in(a,b)+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\frac{|a-b|}{t}

for all intervals
(a,b)
s.t.
0<|a-b|<t
.
Then the convergence is uniform in
(a,b)
.

Proof

Without loss of generality,
(a,b)\subset[0,t)
. We are asked to find for each
\epsilon>0
an
N_{0}
such that

|{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in(a,b)+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})-\tfrac{|a-b|}{t}|<\epsilon\text{ for all $N>N_{0}$ and $a<b$.
}

Choose
0<\delta<\min\{\frac{\epsilon}{5},1\}
, and divide
[0,t]
into finitely many equal disjoint intervals
\{I_{j}\}
with length
|I_{j}|<\delta
. Choose
N_{0}
so that for all
N>N_{0}
, for all
I_{j}
,

\bigl|{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in I_{j}+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})-\frac{|I_{j}|}{t}\bigr|<\frac{\delta|I_{j}|}{t}.

(16.29)

I:=(a,b)
can be approximated from within and from outside by finite (perhaps empty) unions of intervals
I_{j}
whose total length differs from
|a-b|
by no more than
2\delta
. Summing (
16.29
) over these unions we see that for all
N>N_{0}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in I+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})\leq\frac{|a-b|+2\delta}{t}+\frac{\delta(|a-b|+2\delta)}{t}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in I+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})\geq\frac{|a-b|-2\delta}{t}-\frac{\delta|a-b|}{t}.

By choice of
\delta
,
|{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}^{(r)}\in I+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1})-\frac{|a-b|}{t}|<\epsilon
.
\square

We can now prove that
(c)\Rightarrow(a)
.
Suppose
(\mathsf{X}_{r},\mathsf{f}_{r})
has the “mixing uniform distribution mod
t
” property for all
r
and
t
. This property is invariant under centering, because of Lemma
5.69
.
So we may assume without loss of generality that
\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})]=0
for all
n
.

First we claim that
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
is not center-tight. Otherwise there are constants
c_{N}
and
M
such that
{\mathbb{P}}(|S_{N}-c_{N}|>M)<0.1
for all
N
. Take
t:=5M
and
N_{k}\to\infty
such that
c_{N_{k}}\xrightarrow[k\to\infty]{}c\mod t\mathbb{Z}
, then by the bounded convergence theorem and (c),

\displaystyle 0.9

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(S_{N_{k}}\in[c-2M,c+2M]\bigr)\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(S_{N}\in[c-2M,c+2M]+t\mathbb{Z}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}\lim_{N\to\infty}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}\bigl(S_{N}\in[c-2M,c+2M]+t\mathbb{Z}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{S}_{N+1}\bigr)\mu_{1}(dx)={\frac{4M}{t}}=0.8,

a contradiction. Thus
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
is not center-tight and
V_{N}\to\infty
.

Assume by way of contradiction that
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})\neq\mathbb{R}
, then
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=t\mathbb{Z}
for some
t
, and
t\neq 0
because
V_{N}\to\infty
. Without loss of generality
t=1
, otherwise we can rescale
\mathsf{f}
. By the integer reduction lemma, we can write

f_{n}(x,y)+a_{n}(x)-a_{n+1}(y)=\mathbb{F}_{n}(x,y)+h_{n}(x,y)+c_{n}

where
a_{k},\mathbb{F}_{k},h_{k},c_{k}
are uniformly bounded,
\mathbb{F}_{n}
are integer valued,
h_{n}
have summable variances, and
\mathbb{E}(h_{n})=0
. Then
\mathfrak{F}:=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})
converges a.s., and

\mathfrak{F}_{r}:=\sum_{n\geq r}h_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})\xrightarrow[r\to\infty]{}0
almost surely.

Working as in the proof of
(b)\Rightarrow(a)
, we construct
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
and
r>1
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{x}(e^{2\pi i\mathfrak{F}_{r}})|>0.999.

Next we construct a regular sequence of measurable sets
\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}
, and intervals
J_{N}
with lengths
<0.0001
and centers
z_{N}=O(1)
such that

a_{N+1}(X_{N+1})-a_{1}(X_{r})\in J_{N},\text{ whenever }X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1},X_{r}=x.

By Lemma
5.68
with
s=0
,
m=1
, and
v_{N+1}\equiv 1
, there are
c(r,N)\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.

\mathbb{E}_{x}\bigl(e^{2\pi i(S_{N}^{(r)}+a(X_{1})-a(X_{N+1})-z_{N})}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}\bigr)=e^{2\pi i(c(r,N)-z_{N})}\mathbb{E}_{x}(e^{2\pi i\mathfrak{F}_{r}})+o(1),

as
N\to\infty
.
Since

\left\|\left(e^{2\pi i(S_{N}^{(r)}+a(X_{r})-a(X_{N+1})-z_{N})}-e^{2\pi i(S_{N}^{(r)})}\right)1_{[X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1},X_{r}=x]}\right\|_{\infty}<0.1,

we find that for all
N
large enough,

|\mathbb{E}_{x}\bigl(e^{2\pi i(S_{N}^{(r)})}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}\bigr)|>\frac{1}{2}
.

But this is a contradiction, since (c) implies that

\mathbb{E}_{x}\bigl(e^{2\pi iS_{N}^{(r)}}|X_{N+1}\in\mathfrak{A}_{N+1}\bigr)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{iu}du=0.

So
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
and (a) is proved.

\square

16.4
Universal bounds for Markov chains

Lemma 5.70

Suppose
\mathfrak{F}
is a real random variable such that
0\leq\mathfrak{F}<\delta
almost surely.
Then for every interval
(a,b)
of length
L>\delta
,

\left(1-\frac{\delta}{L}\right)|a-b|<\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[1_{(a,b)}(m\delta+\mathfrak{F})]<\left(1+\frac{\delta}{L}\right)|a-b|.

Proof

Fix
k
large, and divide
[0,\delta)
into
k
intervals
I_{j}:=\frac{j\delta}{k}+[0,\frac{\delta}{k})
. For each
j
,

\displaystyle\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[1_{(a,b)}(m\delta+\mathfrak{F})|\mathfrak{F}\in I_{j}]\leq\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[1_{(a+\frac{(j-1)\delta}{k},b+\frac{(j+1)\delta}{k})}(m\delta)|\mathfrak{F}\in I_{j}]

\displaystyle=\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}1_{(a+\frac{(j-1)\delta}{k},b+\frac{(j+1)\delta}{k})}(m\delta)\leq|a-b|+1+\frac{2\delta}{k}\xrightarrow[k\to\infty]{}|a-b|+1.

Multiplying by
{\mathbb{P}}[\mathfrak{F}\in I_{j}]
and summing over
j=0,\ldots,k-1
gives the bound

\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[1_{(a,b)}(m\delta+\mathfrak{F})]\leq|a-b|+\delta.

Similarly,
\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}[1_{(a,b)}(m\delta+\mathfrak{F})]\geq|a-b|-\delta
. The lemma follows.
\square

Proof of Theorem
15.3
:

If
\delta(\mathsf{f})=\infty
then there is nothing to prove, and if
\delta(\mathsf{f})=0
then
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
is non-lattice and irreducible, and the universal bounds follow from Theorem
12.1
. So assume

\delta(\mathsf{f})
is finite and positive.

Suppose
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\to z
.
Let
\mathfrak{F}
and
b_{N}(X_{1},X_{N})
be as in Theorem
15.1
.

Upper bound (
15.6
):
Fix
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
, let
\delta:=\delta(\mathsf{f})
and suppose
(a,b)
is an interval of length
L>\delta
.
We may assume without loss of generality that
a-10\delta,b+10\delta

are not atoms of the distribution of
\mathfrak{F}
given
X_{1}=x
(otherwise change
a,b
a little).

Suppose
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\to z
, and write

z_{N}=\overline{z}_{N}+\zeta_{N},\ \overline{z}_{N}\in\delta\mathbb{Z}\ ,|\zeta_{N}|\leq\delta.

Recall that by Theorem
15.1
,
|b_{N}|\leq 9\delta
.
Therefore

S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)\Rightarrow S_{N}-\overline{z}_{N}-b_{N}\in(a-10\delta,b+10\delta)

So

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-\overline{z}_{N}-b_{N}\in(a-10\delta,b+10\delta)]

\displaystyle=e^{-z^{2}/2}\delta\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[1_{(a-10\delta,b+10\delta)}(m\delta+\mathfrak{F})]\ \ \ \text{by Theorem \ref{Theorem-Reducible-LLT}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\delta}{|a-b|+20\delta}\right)\;e^{-z^{2}/2}(|a-b|+20\delta)\text{ by Lemma \ref{Lemma-HHH}}

(16.30)

\displaystyle\leq\left(|a-b|+21\delta\right)e^{-z^{2}/2}\leq\left(1+\frac{21\delta}{L}\right)e^{-z^{2}/2}|a-b|.

Lower bound (
15.7
):

Fix
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
and an interval
(a,b)
with length bigger than some
L>\delta(\mathsf{f})
.
Recall that
|b_{N}|
are uniformly bounded.
Choose some
{\overline{K}}
so that

{\mathbb{P}}[|b_{N}|\leq{\overline{K}}]=1

and fix
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
s.t.
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\sup|b_{N}|\leq{\overline{K}}]=1
.

Next, divide
[-{\overline{K}},{\overline{K}}]
into
k
disjoint intervals
I_{j,N}
of equal length
\frac{2{\overline{K}}}{k}
,
with
k
large. For each
N
,

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[b_{N}\in I_{j,N}]\geq k^{-2}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[b_{N}\in I_{j,N}]\geq 1-\frac{1}{k},

because to complete the left-hand-side to one we need to add the probabilities of
[b_{N}\in I_{j,N}]
for the
j
s.t.
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[b_{N}\in I_{j,N}]<k^{-2}
, and there are at most
k
such events.

Therefore, we can divide
\{I_{j,N}\}
into two groups of size at most
k
: The first contains the
I_{j,N}
with
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[b_{N}\in I_{j,N}]\geq k^{-2}
, and the second corresponds to events with total probability less than
\frac{1}{k}
(conditioned on
X_{1}=x
).

Re-index the intervals in the first group (perhaps with repetitions) in such a way that it takes the form
I_{j,N}

(j=1,\ldots,k)
for all
N
. Then
for each
j
,
\mathfrak{A}_{j,N}:=[b_{N}\in I_{j,N},X_{1}=x]
is a regular sequence of events.

Let
\beta_{j,N}:=
center of
I_{j,N}
and set
z_{j,N}:=z_{N}-\beta_{j,N}
.
Every sequence has a subsequence s.t.
z_{j,N}

converges mod
\delta(\mathsf{f})
. We will henceforth assume that
z_{j,N}=\overline{z}_{j,N}+\zeta_{0}+\zeta_{j,N}
where
\overline{z}_{j,N}\in\delta(\mathsf{f})\mathbb{Z}
and
|\zeta_{j,N}|<\frac{K}{k}
, and
|\zeta_{0}|<\delta(\mathsf{f})
is fixed.

Recall that
|I_{j,N}|=\frac{2{\overline{K}}}{k}
. Conditioned on
\mathfrak{A}_{j,N}
,
b_{N}=\beta_{j,N}\pm\frac{2{\overline{K}}}{k}
, therefore
\overline{z}_{j,N}+\zeta_{0}+b_{N}=z_{N}\pm\frac{3K}{k}
, whence

S_{N}-\overline{z}_{j,N}-b_{N}\in\left(a-\zeta_{0}+\frac{3{\overline{K}}}{k},b-\zeta_{0}-\frac{3{\overline{K}}}{k}\right)\Rightarrow S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b).

There is no loss of generality in assuming that the endpoints of this interval are not atoms of the distribution of
\mathfrak{F}
given
X_{1}=x
, otherwise perturb
K
a little. Since
\mathfrak{A}_{j,N}
is a regular sequence, we have by Theorem
15.1
part (2) and the lemma that

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)|\mathfrak{A}_{j,N})

\displaystyle\geq\liminf\limits_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}-\overline{z}_{j,N}-b_{N}\in(a-\zeta_{0}+\tfrac{3{\overline{K}}}{k},b-\zeta_{0}-\tfrac{3{\overline{K}}}{k})|\mathfrak{A}_{j,N})

\displaystyle=\delta(\mathsf{f})e^{-z^{2}/2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[1_{(a-\zeta_{0}+\frac{3{\overline{K}}}{k},b-\zeta_{0}-\frac{3{\overline{K}}}{k})}(m\delta(\mathsf{f})+\mathfrak{F})]

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{L}\right)\bigl(|a-b|-\tfrac{6{\overline{K}}}{k}\bigr)e^{-z^{2}/2}.

(16.31)

We now multiply these bounds by
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\mathfrak{A}_{j,N}]
and sum over
j
. This gives

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}\left(\left[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)\right]\bigcap\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\mathfrak{A}_{j,N}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{L}\right)\left(|a-b|-\tfrac{6{\overline{K}}}{k}\right)e^{-z^{2}/2}\left(1-\frac{1}{k}\right).

Passing to the limit
k\to\infty
, we obtain

\liminf\limits_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}\left(\left[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)\right]\right)\geq\left(1-\frac{\delta}{L}\right)e^{-z^{2}/2}|a-b|,

and the lower bound is proved.

To prove the last statement of the theorem let

\mathcal{A}_{x}

be the positive functional on
C_{c}(\mathbb{R})
defined by (
15.4
), and let

\mu_{\mathcal{A}_{x}}
be the Radon measure on
\mathbb{R}
s.t.
\mu_{\mathcal{A}_{x}}(\phi)=\mathcal{A}_{x}[\phi]
for
\phi\in C_{c}(\mathbb{R})
.

The inequalities (
16.30
), (
16.31
) can be used to see that

(1-\delta L^{-1})(|a-b|-O(1))\leq\mu_{\mathcal{A}_{x}}(a,b)\leq(1+21\delta L^{-1})(|a-b|+O(1)),

whence
\displaystyle\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{\mathcal{A}}[0,L]}{L}=1.
Since
\mu_{\mathcal{A}_{x}}
is clearly invariant under translation by
\delta(\mathsf{f})
, it must be the case that for each
a
,
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}[a,a+\delta(\mathsf{f}))=\delta(\mathsf{f})
, whence

\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\;\;\mu_{\mathcal{A}}([a,a+\delta(k\mathsf{f})))=k\delta(\mathsf{f}).

(16.32)

Given an interval
(a,b)
of length
L
with
k\delta(\mathsf{f})<L<(k+1)\delta(\mathsf{f})

take two intervals
I^{-},I^{+}
such that

I^{-}\subset(a,b)\subset I^{+},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial I^{-})=\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial I^{+})=0,\hskip 11.74988pt|I^{-}|=k\delta(\mathsf{f}),\hskip 11.74988pt|I^{+}|=(k+1)\delta(\mathsf{f}).

Next let
\phi^{-},\phi^{+}
be continuous functions with compact support such that

1_{I^{-}}<\phi^{-}<1_{[a,b]}<\phi^{+}<1_{I^{+}}.

Then for large
N
,

\sqrt{V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
is sandwiched between

\mathcal{A}(\phi^{-})
and
\mathcal{A}(\phi^{+})
which in turn is sandwiched between

\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(I^{-})={k\delta(\mathsf{f})}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{and}\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(I^{+})=(k+1)\delta(\mathsf{f})

where the equalities rely on
(
16.32
). The proof of the theorem is complete.

\Box

16.5
Universal bounds for Markov arrays

Next, we give a different proof of universal lower and upper bounds, which does not rely on Theorem
15.1
, and which also applies to arrays
and to arbitrary initial distributions.

Theorem 16.1

Let
\mathsf{X}
be a uniformly elliptic Markov array, and
\mathsf{f}
an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional which is stably hereditary and not center tight. For every
\epsilon>0
there is
N_{\epsilon}>0
as follows. Suppose
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}z\in\mathbb{R}
, and
|a-b|>2\delta(\mathsf{f})+\epsilon
, then for all
N>N_{\epsilon}
,

\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))\leq 3\left(\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\right).

Recall that by our conventions, the
Fourier transform
of an
L^{1}
function
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is

\widehat{\gamma}(x)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-itx}\gamma(t)dt.

Fix some
b>0
, and define the Fourier pair

\psi_{b}(t):=\frac{\pi}{4b}1_{[-b,b]}(t)\ ,\ \widehat{\psi}_{b}(x)=\frac{\pi}{2b}\left(\frac{\sin(bx)}{x}\right).

Lemma 5.71

1\leq\widehat{\psi_{b}}(x)\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
for
|x|\leq\frac{\pi}{2b}
;
and
|\widehat{\psi_{b}}(x)|<1
for
|x|>\frac{\pi}{2b}
.

Proof

The function
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(x)
is even, with zeroes at
z_{n}=\pi n/b
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
.
The critical points are
c_{0}=0
and
\pm c_{n}
where
n\geq 1
and

c_{n}:=\text{the unique solution of $\tan(bc_{n})=bc_{n}$ in $\left(z_{n},z_{n}+\frac{\pi}{2b}\right)$}.

It is easy to see that
c_{n}=z_{n}+\frac{\pi}{2b}-o(1)
as
n\to\infty
, and that

\mathrm{sgn}[\widehat{\psi_{b}}(c_{n})]=(-1)^{n}\ ,|\widehat{\psi_{b}}(c_{n})|\leq\frac{1}{2n}\ ,\ \widehat{\psi_{b}}(c_{n})\sim\frac{(-1)^{n}}{2n}\text{ as }n\to\infty.

So
\widehat{\psi_{b}}
attains global maximum
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(0)=\frac{\pi}{2}
at
c_{0}
, and
|\widehat{\psi_{b}}(t)|\leq\frac{1}{2n}
everywhere on
[\pi n/b,\pi(n+1)/b]
.

In particular,
|\widehat{\psi_{b}}(t)|<1/2
for
|t|\geq\pi/b
.
On
(0,\pi/b)
the function is decreasing from its global maximum
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(0)=\frac{\pi}{2}
to
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(\frac{\pi}{b})=0
, passing through
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(\frac{\pi}{2b})=1
. It follows that
1\leq\widehat{\psi_{b}}(t)\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
on
(0,\frac{\pi}{2b})
and
|\widehat{\psi_{b}}(t)|<1
for
t>\frac{\pi}{2b}
. The lemma follows, because
\widehat{\psi_{b}}(-t)=\widehat{\psi_{b}}(t)
.
\square

Lemma 5.72

There exist two continuous functions
\gamma_{1}(x),\gamma_{2}(x)
s.t.

{\rm supp}(\gamma_{i})\subset[-2,2]
;

{\gamma}_{1}(0)>\frac{1}{3}
;
{\gamma}_{2}(0)<3
; and

\widehat{\gamma}_{1}(x)\leq 1_{[-\pi,\pi]}(x)\leq\widehat{\gamma}_{2}(x)\ \ \ (x\in\mathbb{R}).

Proof

Throughout this proof,
\psi^{\ast n}:=\psi\ast\cdots\ast\psi
(
n
times), where
\ast
denotes the convolution.
Let
\gamma_{1}(t):=\frac{1}{4}[\psi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\ast 4}(t)-\psi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\ast 2}(t)]
.
Then

\widehat{\gamma}_{1}(x)=\frac{1}{4}[\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}}(x)^{4}-\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}}(x)^{2}].

By Lemma
5.71
,
1\leq\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
on
[-\pi,\pi]
and
|\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}}|<1
outside
[-\pi,\pi]
. So

\displaystyle\max_{|x|\leq\pi}\widehat{\gamma}_{1}(x)\leq\max_{1\leq y\leq\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{1}{4}(y^{4}-y^{2})=\frac{1}{4}\left[\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{4}-\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{2}\right]<1,

\displaystyle\max_{|x|\geq\pi}\widehat{\gamma}_{1}(x)\leq\max_{|y|\leq 1}\frac{1}{4}(y^{4}-y^{2})=0.

So
\widehat{\gamma}_{1}(x)\leq 1_{[-\pi,\pi]}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

It is obvious from the definition of the convolution that

{\rm supp}(\gamma_{1})=\{x+y+z+w:x,y,z,w\in[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]\}=[-2,2].

Here is the calculation showing that
\gamma_{1}(0)>\frac{1}{3}
:

\displaystyle(\psi_{b}^{\ast 2})(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2}}{16b^{2}}(1_{[-b,b]}\ast 1_{[-b,b]})(t)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{16b^{2}}1_{[-2b,2b]}(t)(2b-|t|)

\displaystyle(\psi_{b}^{\ast 4})(0)

\displaystyle=(\psi_{b}^{\ast 2}\ast\psi_{b}^{\ast 2})(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{4}}{256b^{4}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}1_{[-2b,2b]}(t)(2b-|t|)1_{[-2b,2b]}(-t)(2b-|-t|)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{4}}{256b^{4}}\int_{-2b}^{2b}(2b-|t|)^{2}dt=\frac{\pi^{4}}{128b^{4}}\int_{0}^{2b}(2b-t)^{2}dt=\frac{\pi^{4}}{128b^{4}}\cdot\frac{(2b)^{3}}{3}=\frac{\pi^{4}}{48b}.

So
\psi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\ast 4}(0)=\frac{\pi^{4}}{24}
,
\psi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\ast 2}(0)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}
, and

\gamma_{1}(0)=\frac{1}{4}(\frac{\pi^{4}}{24}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4})>\frac{1}{3}
.

Next we set

\gamma_{2}(t):=(\psi_{\frac{1}{2}}\ast\psi_{\frac{1}{2}})(t)\equiv\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}1_{[-1,1]}(t)(1-|t|).

Then
{\rm supp}(\gamma_{2})=[-1,1]
and
\gamma_{2}(0)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}<3
. Finally,
\widehat{\gamma}_{2}\geq 1_{[-\pi,\pi]}(x)
, because by Lemma
5.71
,

\circ

\widehat{\gamma}_{2}(t)=(\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}})^{2}(x)\geq 1
for all
|x|\leq\frac{\pi}{2\cdot\frac{1}{2}}=\pi
, and

\circ

\widehat{\gamma}_{2}(t)=(\widehat{\psi}_{\frac{1}{2}})^{2}(x)\geq 0
for all
|x|\geq\pi
.
\square

Proof of Theorem
16.1
.

If
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
then the theorem follows from the LLT in the irreducible case. Otherwise (since
\mathsf{f}
is not center-tight),
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=t\mathbb{Z}
for some
t>0
, and there is no loss of generality in assuming that
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
.

Henceforth we assume that
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
. In this case our interval
I:=[a,b]
has length bigger than
2
. Notice that we can always center
I
by modifying
z_{N}
by a constant. So we may take our interval to be of the form

I=[-a,a],\text{ with }a>1.

Let
\gamma_{i}(t)
be the functions constructed in Lemma
5.72
, then

\widehat{\gamma}_{1}\left(\frac{\pi t}{a}\right)\leq 1_{I}(t)\leq\widehat{\gamma}_{2}\left(\frac{\pi t}{a}\right).

Therefore,
for every choice of
x^{(N)}_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{1}

(N\geq 1)
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in I)=\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}[1_{I}(S_{N}-z_{N})]\geq\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}\left[\widehat{\gamma}_{1}\left(\frac{\pi(S_{N}-z_{N})}{a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-i\frac{\pi t}{a}(S_{N}-z_{N})}\gamma_{1}(t)dt\right]=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\frac{\pi t}{a}(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma_{1}(t)dt.

Recalling that
{\rm supp}(\gamma_{1})\subset[-2,2]
, and substituting
t=a\xi/\pi
, we obtain

{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in I)\geq\frac{|I|}{2\pi}\int_{-2\pi/a}^{2\pi/a}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma_{1}(\tfrac{a\xi}{\pi})d\xi.

(16.33)

Similarly, we have

{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in I)\leq\frac{|I|}{2\pi}\int_{-2\pi/a}^{2\pi/a}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma_{2}(\tfrac{a\xi}{\pi})d\xi.

(16.34)

Next we claim that under the assumptions of
Theorem
16.1
:

Lemma 5.73

If
G_{ess}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
and

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}z\in\mathbb{R}
, then for every
a>1

\sqrt{V_{N}}\int\limits_{-2\pi/a}^{2\pi/a}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma_{i}\left(\tfrac{a\xi}{\pi}\right)d\xi\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{2}}\gamma_{i}(0)

and the convergence is uniform in
a
on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]
.

Proof.

In what follows we fix
i\in\{1,2\}
and let

\displaystyle\gamma(\xi):=\gamma_{i}\left(\tfrac{a\xi}{\pi}\right)
.
Divide
[-\frac{2\pi}{a},\frac{2\pi}{a}]
into segments
I_{j}
of length at most
\widetilde{\delta}
, where
\widetilde{\delta}
is given by Lemma
5.68
, making sure that
I_{0}
is centered at zero. Let

J_{j,N}:=\int_{I_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma(\xi)d\xi.

Claim 1.

\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{0,N}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-z^{2}/2}\gamma(0)
.

Proof.
The proof is similar to the proof of (
13.17
).

Applying Corollary
4.62
to the interval
I_{0}
,
and noting that
A_{N}(I_{0})=0
and
\widetilde{\xi}_{N}=0
we find that

|\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})|\leq\widetilde{C}\exp(-\widehat{{\varepsilon}}\xi^{2}V_{N}).

So for every
R>1
,

\sqrt{V_{N}}\int_{\xi\in I_{0}:|\xi|>\frac{R}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}}\;\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma(\xi)d\xi=O(e^{-\widehat{{\varepsilon}}R^{2}}).

Similarly, for all
N
large enough

\displaystyle\sqrt{V_{N}}\int_{\xi\in I_{0}:|\xi|\leq\frac{R}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})\gamma(\xi)d\xi=\int_{-R}^{R}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\eta(\frac{S_{N}-z_{N}}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})})\gamma(\tfrac{\eta}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})d\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{-R}^{R}\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\eta(\frac{S_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})})e^{i\eta(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})}\gamma_{i}(\tfrac{a\eta}{\pi\sqrt{V_{N}}})d\eta

\displaystyle\overset{!}{=}\int_{-R}^{R}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{2}+i\eta z}\gamma(0)d\eta+o_{N\to\infty}(1)\text{ uniformly on compact sets of $a$ }

\displaystyle{=}\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{2}}\gamma(0)+o_{R\to\infty}(1)+o_{N\to\infty}(1),

where
\overset{!}{=}
is a consequence of Dobrushin’s CLT and the bounded convergence theorem. (When applying Dobrushin’s Theorem it is useful to recall that by the exponential mixing of uniformly elliptic arrays,
|\mathbb{E}(S_{N})-\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N})|=O(1)
, therefore the condition
\frac{z-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\to z
is equivalent to the condition
(z-\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N}))/\sqrt{V_{N}}\to z
.)
In summary,

\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{0,N}=\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{2}}\gamma(0)+o_{R\to\infty}(1)+o_{N\to\infty}(1).

Fixing
R
, we see that
\limsup\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{0,N}
and

\liminf\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{0,N}
are both equal to

\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{2}}\gamma(0)+o_{R\to\infty}(1).

Passing to the limit
R\to\infty
gives us that the limit exists and is equal to
\sqrt{2\pi}e^{-\frac{1}{2}z^{2}}\gamma(0)
.

It is not difficult to see that the convergence is uniform on compact subsets of
a
.

Claim 2.

\sqrt{V_{N}}J_{j,N}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0
for every
j\neq 0
.

Proof.
Since
G_{ess}(\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
, the co-range is
H(\mathsf{f})=2\pi\mathbb{Z}
. So

I_{j}\subset[-\tfrac{2\pi}{a},\tfrac{2\pi}{a}]\setminus\mathrm{int}(I_{0})\subset\text{ a compact subset of }\mathbb{R}\setminus H(\mathsf{f}).

This implies by the stable hereditary property of
\mathsf{f}
that

D_{N}(\xi)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\infty\text{ uniformly on }I_{j},

whence by (
13.6
),

|\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\eta(S_{N}-z_{N})})|\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0
uniformly on
I_{j}
.

Let
A_{j,N}:=-\log\{\sup|\mathbb{E}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(e^{-i\xi(S_{N}-z_{N})})|:(x,\xi)\in\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{1}\times I_{j}\}
, then

A_{j,N}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\infty
, and this divergence is uniform for
a
ranging over compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]
.

From this point onward, the proof of the claim is identical to the proof of (
13.18
). We omit the details.

The Lemma follows by summing over all subintervals
I_{j}
in
[-\frac{2\pi}{a},\frac{2\pi}{a}]
, and noting that the number of these intervals is uniformly bounded

\left(\text{by }1+\frac{4\pi}{\widetilde{\delta}}\right).

\Box

We now return to the proof of theorem.
Lemma
5.73
, (
16.33
), (
16.34
), and the inequalities
\gamma_{1}(0)>\frac{1}{3}
and
\gamma_{2}(0)<3
imply that for every choice of
\{x^{(N)}_{1}\}_{N\geq 1}
, for all
N
sufficiently large

\frac{1}{3}\cdot\frac{|I|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}e^{-z^{2}/2}\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{(N)}_{1}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in I)\leq 3\cdot\frac{|I|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}e^{-z^{2}/2}.

(16.35)

This estimate is uniform in
\{x^{(N)}_{1}\}_{N\geq 1}
: There is an
N_{0}
such that (
16.36
) holds for all
N\geq N_{0}
and
for all
choices of
\{x^{(N)}_{1}\}_{N\geq 1}
. Otherwise, there exist
N_{k}\to\infty
and
x^{(N_{k})}_{1}\in\mathfrak{S}^{(N_{k})}_{1}
which violate (
16.36
). But then (
16.36
) fails for any choice of
x^{(N)}_{1}
which contains
x^{(N_{k})}_{1}
as a subsequence, whereas (
16.36
) holds for all possible choices.

Since (
16.36
) holds uniformly in
\{x^{(N_{k})}_{1}\}_{N\geq 1}
, we can integrate and deduce that
for all
N
sufficiently large

\frac{1}{3}\cdot\frac{|I|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}e^{-z^{2}/2}\leq{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in I)\leq 3\cdot\frac{|I|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}e^{-z^{2}/2}

(16.36)

for
any
initial distributions
\mu^{(N)}_{1}(dx_{1}^{(N)})
on
\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{1}
.
\Box

We end this section by recording a useful consequence of the previous proof: The upper
bound in Theorem
16.1
does not require any information about the arithmetic properties of
\mathsf{f}
.

Lemma 5.74

For each
K,{\varepsilon}_{0}
and
\ell
there is a constant
C^{*}=C^{*}(K,{\varepsilon}_{0},\ell)
s.t. if
\mathsf{f}

is an additive functional of a uniformly elliptic Markov chain with ellipticity constant
{\varepsilon}_{0}
, and if

|\mathsf{f}|\leq K
, then for every
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,
N\geq 1
, and for each interval
J
of length
\ell
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}\left(S_{N}\in J\right)\leq\frac{C^{*}}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}.

Proof

It suffices to prove the result for
\ell=4
since longer intervals could be covered by a finite number
of intervals of length 4. Thus
J=z_{N}+I
with
I=[-2,2].
Applying (
16.34
) with
a=2
we get

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(S_{N}\in J)\leq{\widehat{C}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left|\Phi_{N}(x,-\xi)\right|d\xi.

where
\displaystyle{\widehat{C}}=\frac{2}{\pi}\|\gamma_{2}\|_{\infty}.

Dividing
[-\pi,\pi]
into finitely many subintervals of length
{\widetilde{\delta}}/2
where

{\widetilde{\delta}}
comes from Lemma
4.61
,
and applying (
13.19
) on each subinterval we obtain the result.

\hfill\Box

17
Notes and references

Dolgopyat proved a version of Theorem
15.1
for sums of independent random variables.
The connection between the LLT and uniform distribution modulo
t
was considered for sums of independent random variables by Prohorov
Prohorov
,
Rozanov
Rozanov
,
and Gamkrelidze
Gamkrelidze
.

The question of estimating
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
is related to the study of the rate of
convergence in the CLT. In particular,
a Berry-Esseen type result on the rate of convergence in the CLT would certainly imply that
\exists M
s.t. for all
|a-b|>M
, if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\to z
, then for all
N
large enough,

{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
equals
\frac{e^{-z^{2}/2}|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}
up to bounded multiplicative error. Such results were shown to us by Y. Hafouta.
The Berry-Esseen approach has the advantage of gives information on the time
N
when the universal estimates kick in, but has the disadvantage that it only applies to very large intervals (how large depends on the growth of the third moment of
S_{N}
). By contrast, the results of this chapter apply to intervals of length
>\delta(\mathsf{f})
, which is optimal, but do not say on how large
N
should be for the estimates to work.

Lemma
5.74
for the sums of independent random variables appears in
(
Petrov-Book
, Section III.1)
. The proof in the Markov case is essentially the same.

Chapter 6
Local limit theorems for large and moderate deviations

In this chapter we prove the local limit theorem in the regimes of moderate and large deviations. In these cases the asymptotic behavior of
{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
is determined by the “rate functions,” the Legendre transforms of the log-moment generating functions of
S_{N}
.

18
The moderate deviations and large deviations regimes

Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an irreducible, a.s. uniformly bounded, additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov
chain

\mathsf{X}
, with algebraic range
\mathbb{R}
or
t\mathbb{Z}
with
t>0
. Let

S_{N}=f_{1}(X_{1},X_{2})+\cdots+f_{N}(X_{N},X_{N+1})\ ,\ V_{N}:={\rm Var}(S_{N}).

In the previous chapters, we analyzed
{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
as
N\to\infty
, in the
regime of local deviations
,
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}}\to const
.
In this chapter we ask what happens
when

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}}\to\infty
.

Usually in the literature the large deviations regime is defined by the condition

|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|\geq{\overline{{\varepsilon}}}{\rm Var}(S_{N})
for some fixed
{\overline{{\varepsilon}}}>0
. However, to get meaningful results we need
to assume some upper bounds
|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|
as well.

We will study the following regimes:

(1)

Moderate deviations:

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}}\to\infty
and
z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})=o({\rm Var}(S_{N})),

(2)

Large deviations:

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}}\to\infty
and
|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|\leq\epsilon{\rm Var}(S_{N})
for some
\epsilon>0
“small
enough.”

In some cases we can take
{\varepsilon}=\infty
, see e.g. §
25
, but in others
{\varepsilon}
must really be finite, see Example
6.95
.
To see why it is forced on us, let us consider a few examples of what might go wrong when
|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|/{\rm Var}(S_{N})
is “too big.”

If
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
grows too fast, e.g. if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}>\frac{2\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|S_{N}|}{V_{N}}
, then the probabilities
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(0,\infty)]
are all equal to zero, and our problem is vacuous.
A more subtle but related issue arises when
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
falls at the boundary of the domain of the Legendre transforms of
t\mapsto\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{t(S_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N}))})
. Why this matters will be clear once we explain the strategy of our proofs (see the end of §
20.1
and §
21
). At this point we can only present an example:

Example 6.75

If
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
falls near the boundary of the domain of the Legendre transforms of
t\mapsto\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{t(S_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N}))})
, then the behavior of
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
may depend not just on
\lim\limits_{N\to\infty}\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
but also on
z_{N}
itself.

Proof

Let
S_{N}:=X_{1}+\cdots+X_{N}
where
X_{i}
are identically distributed independent random variables equal to
-1,0,1
with equal probabilities.
Here
\mathbb{E}(S_{N})=0
,
V_{N}=2N/3
, the Legendre transforms of the log-moment generating functions have domains
(-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2})
, and the classical theory of large deviations says that if
z\in(-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2})
, then
\displaystyle\lim_{z_{N}/V_{N}\to z}\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}>0]
exists and is finite.
But no such conclusion holds when
z=\frac{3}{2}
:

\circ

If
z_{N}=N
, then
[S_{N}-z_{N}>0]=\varnothing
and

\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}>0]=-\infty
;

\circ

If
z_{N}=N-1
, then
[S_{N}-z_{N}>0]=[S_{N}=N]
, and
\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}>0]=-\frac{3}{2}\log 3
.

So the limit depends on how
z_{N}/V_{N}
approaches

\frac{3}{2}
, and it could be infinite.
\square

For general additive functionals on Markov chains (homogeneous or not),
we do not know how to determine the asymptotic behavior of
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
when
\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}
is close to
\partial C_{N}
, where

C_{N}:=\text{domain of the Legendre transform of $\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{t(S_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N}))})$.}

We can only analyze the case where

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}

is well inside the interior of
C_{N}
for all
N
. This is why we must assume that
|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|\leq{\varepsilon}{\rm Var}(S_{N})
for
{\varepsilon}
small enough.

It is instructive to compare the regime of large deviations to the regime of the LLT from the point of view of universality.

The asymptotic behavior
of
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
in the regime of local deviations does not depend on the details of the distributions of
f_{n}(X_{n},X_{n+1})
. It depends only on rough features such as
{\rm Var}(S_{N})
, the algebraic range, and (in case the algebraic range is
t\mathbb{Z}
) on the constants
c_{N}
s.t.
S_{N}\in c_{N}+t\mathbb{Z}
almost surely.

By contrast, in the regime of large deviations the asymptotic behavior of
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
depends on the entire distribution of
S_{N}
. The dependence is through the Legendre transform of
\log\mathbb{E}(e^{tS_{N}})
, a function which encodes the entire distribution of
S_{N}
, not just its rough features.

We will consider two partial remedies to the lack of universality:

(a)

Conditioning:
The conditional distributions of
S_{N}-z_{n}
given
that
S_{N}-z_{N}>a
has a universal scaling limit, see Corollary
6.78
.

(b)

Moderate deviations:

If
|z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})|=o({\rm Var}(S_{N}))
, then
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]
have universal lower and upper bounds (Theorems
19.3
,
19.4
).

19
Local limit theorems for large deviations

19.1
The log moment generating functions

Suppose
|{\mathsf{f}}|<K
almost surely. For every
N
such that
V_{N}\neq 0
, we define the
normalized log moment generating function
of
S_{N}
to be

\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi):=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})\ \ \ \ (\xi\in\mathbb{R}).

The a.s. uniform boundedness of
\mathsf{f}
guarantees the finiteness of the expectation, and the real analyticity of
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)
on
\mathbb{R}
.

Example 6.76
(Sums of iid’s)

Suppose that
\displaystyle S_{N}=\sum_{n=1}^{N}X_{n}
where
X_{n}
where
X_{N}
are i.i.d. bounded random variables
with non-zero variance.
Let
X
denote the common law of

X_{n}.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}_{X}(\xi):=\frac{1}{{\rm Var}(X)}\log{\mathbb{E}}(e^{\xi X})

is independent of
n.
In addition,

(i)

\mathfs F_{X}(\xi)
is strictly convex, by Hölder’s inequality and because
X\neq const
a.s. Since
\mathfs F_{X}(\xi)
is smooth, its second derivative must be
bounded away from
zero on compacts. So
\mathfs F_{N}(\xi)
are uniformly strictly convex on compacts.

(ii)

\displaystyle\lim_{\xi\to-\infty}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\mathrm{ess\,}\inf(X)/{\rm Var}(X),

\displaystyle\lim_{\xi\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\mathrm{ess\,}\sup(X)/{\rm Var}(X).

To see this, use convexity to see that
\lim\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)
are the slopes of the asymptotes of
\mathfs F_{X}(\xi)
, or equivalently

\lim\frac{1}{\xi}\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)
. The last limits can be easily found to be equal to
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup(X)/{\rm Var}(X)
as
\xi\to\infty
, and
\mathrm{ess\,}\inf(X)/{\rm Var}(X)
as
\xi\to-\infty
.

Properties (i) and (ii) play a key role in the study of large deviations for sums of i.i.d. random variables.
A significant part of the effort in this chapter is to understand to which extent similar results holds
in the setting of bounded additive functionals of uniformly elliptic Markov chains.
We start with the following facts.

Theorem 19.1

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional
of a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
, and assume
V_{N}\neq 0
for all
N\geq N_{0}
, then

(1)

For all
N\geq N_{0}
,
\mathcal{F}_{N}(0)=0\ ,\ \mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\ ,\ \mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(0)=1
.

(2)

For every
N\geq N_{0}
,
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)
is strictly convex on
\mathbb{R}
.

(3)

The convexity is uniform on compacts: For every
R>0
there is
C=C(R)
positive s.t. for all
N\geq N_{0}
,
C^{-1}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\leq C
on
[-R,R]
.

(4)

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
. For every
\epsilon>0
there are
\delta,N_{\epsilon}>0
s.t. for all
|\xi|\leq\delta
,
N>N_{\epsilon}
, we have
e^{-\epsilon}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\leq e^{\epsilon}
, and

e^{-\epsilon}\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\xi\leq e^{\epsilon}\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}.

This is very similar to what happens for iid’s, but there is one important difference: In our setting
V_{N}
may be much smaller than
N.

For the proof of this theorem see §
20.5
. Here is an immediate corollary:

Corollary 6.77

Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
. If
V_{N}:={\rm Var}(S_{N})\to\infty
, then for all
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
and
\kappa>0
, if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\sim\kappa V_{N}^{-\alpha}
as
N\to\infty
, then

\lim\limits_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{V_{N}^{2\alpha-1}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\geq 0]=-\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}.

Proof

There is no loss of generality in assuming that
\mathbb{E}(S_{N})=0
for all
N
.
Let
a_{n}:=V_{n}^{1-2\alpha}
,
b_{n}:=V_{n}^{\alpha}
,
W_{n}:=S_{n}/b_{n}
. Then
a_{n}\to\infty
, whence by
Theorem
19.1
(4),

\mathfs F(\xi):=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{a_{n}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi W_{n}})=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}V_{n}^{2\alpha}\mathfs F_{N}(\frac{\xi}{V_{n}^{\alpha}})=\frac{1}{2}\xi^{2}.

We may now use the Gärtner-Ellis Theorem see e.g.
(
El
, Thm II.6.1)
) and
\frac{z_{n}}{a_{n}b_{n}}\to\kappa
to deduce that

\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{a_{n}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[{S_{n}-z_{n}}\geq 0]=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{a_{n}}\log{\mathbb{P}}[\frac{W_{n}}{a_{n}}\geq\frac{z_{n}}{a_{n}b_{n}}]=-\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}
.
\square

19.2
The rate functions

Suppose
V_{N}\neq 0
.
The
rate functions

\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)
are the
Legendre transforms
of
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)
. Specifically,
let
a_{N}:=\inf\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}
and
b_{N}:=\sup\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}
; then
\mathcal{I}_{N}:(a_{N},b_{N})\to\mathbb{R}
is

\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta):=\xi\eta-\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)\text{ for the unique $\xi$ s.t. $\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\eta$}.

The existence and uniqueness of
\xi
is because of the smoothness and strict convexity of
\mathcal{F}_{N}
on
\mathbb{R}
.
We call
(a_{N},b_{N})
the
domain
of
\mathcal{I}_{N}
, and denote it by

\mathrm{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{N}):=(a_{N},b_{N}).

Equivalently,
\mathrm{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{N})=(\mathfs F^{\prime}(-\infty),\mathfs F^{\prime}(+\infty))
, where
\mathfs F^{\prime}(\pm\infty):=\lim\limits_{t\to\pm\infty}\mathfs F^{\prime}(t)
.
Later we will also need the sets
(a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R})\subset\mathrm{dom}(\mathfs I_{N})
, where
R>0
and

a_{N}^{R}:=\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-R),\hskip 11.74988ptb_{N}^{R}:=\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(R).

(19.1)

The functions
\mathfs I_{N}
and their domains depend on
N
.
The following theorem identifies certain uniformity and universality in their behavior.

Theorem 19.2

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
, and assume
V_{N}\neq 0
for all
N
large enough, then

(1)

\exists c,N_{1},R>0
s.t. for all
N>N_{1}
,

\mathrm{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{N})\supset[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]\supseteq\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-c,\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+c\right].

(2)

For each
R
there exists
\rho=\rho(R)
s.t.

\rho^{-1}\leq\mathcal{I}_{N}^{\prime\prime}\leq\rho
on
[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]
for all
N>N_{1}
.

(3)

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
. For every
\epsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0
and
N_{\epsilon}
such that for all
\eta\in[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-\delta,\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+\delta]
and
N>N_{\delta}
,

e^{-\epsilon}\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)\leq e^{\epsilon}\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}.

(4)

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
and
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then

V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)=\frac{1+o(1)}{2}\left(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\right)^{2}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{as}\hskip 11.74988ptN\to\infty.

The proof of the theorem will be given in §
20.6
.

The significance of part (4) will become apparent in §
19.3
.

19.3
The LLT for moderate deviations.

Recall that the state spaces of
\mathsf{X}
are denoted by
\mathfrak{S}_{i}

(i\geq 1)
, and that
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}
denotes the conditional probability given
X_{1}=x
.

Theorem 19.3

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
. Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible with algebraic range
\mathbb{R}
. If
z_{N}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then for every non-empty
(a,b)
and
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]=[1+o(1)]\frac{|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp\left(-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)\right)\text{as $N\to\infty$,}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]=[1+o(1)]\frac{|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp\left[-\frac{1+o(1)}{2}\left(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\right)^{2}\right]\text{ as $N\to\infty$.}

Theorem 19.4

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
.
Assume
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible with algebraic range
\mathbb{Z}
, and
S_{N}\in c_{N}+\mathbb{Z}
almost surely. If
z_{N}\in c_{N}+\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then for every
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}=z_{N}]=\frac{[1+o(1)]}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp\left(-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)\right)\text{ as $N\to\infty$,}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}=z_{N}]=\frac{[1+o(1)]}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp\left[-\frac{1+o(1)}{2}\left(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\right)^{2}\right]\text{ as $N\to\infty$.}

We will obtain these results as special cases of a more complicated and general asymptotic relation which we will state in the next section.

The two asymptotic relations in Theorems
19.3
and
19.4
complement each other. The first is a precise asymptotic, but it is not universal, because it is expressed in terms of the rate functions, which depend on the fine details of the distributions of
S_{N}
.
The second is universal, but it is not an asymptotic equivalence because the right-hand-side is only determined up to a multiplicative error of size
\exp[o(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})^{2}]
.

19.4
The LLT for large deviations.

Recall the definition of the subsets
(a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}):=(\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-R),\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(R))\subset\mathrm{dom}(\mathfs I_{N})
from (
19.1
). It is convenient to define

[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]:=\left[a_{N}^{R}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}},b_{N}^{R}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right].

Theorem 19.5

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded, irreducible, additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
.
For every
R
large enough there are functions

\displaystyle\rho_{N}:\mathfrak{S}_{1}\times\left[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}\right]\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\xi_{N}:[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]\to\mathbb{R}

as follows:

(1)

\exists c>0
such that
[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]\supset[-c,c]
for all
N
large enough.

(2)

Non Lattice case:
Suppose
G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
, then for every sequence of
z_{N}\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
,
for all finite non-empty intervals
(a,b)
, and for every
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,
we have the following asymptotic as
N\to\infty
:

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]=[1+o(1)]\cdot\frac{e^{-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}})}}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\rho_{N}\left(x,\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)\int_{a}^{b}e^{-t\xi_{N}\left(\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)}dt.

(3)

Lattice case:
Suppose
G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{Z}
and
S_{N}\in c_{N}+\mathbb{Z}
a.s., then
for every sequence of
z_{N}\in c_{N}+\mathbb{Z}
s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
,
for all finite non-empty intervals
(a,b)
and
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
, the following asymptotic holds when
N\to\infty
:

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]=[1+o(1)]\cdot\frac{e^{-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}})}}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\rho_{N}\left(x,\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)\cdot\sum_{t\in(a,b)\cap\mathbb{Z}}e^{-t\xi_{N}\left(\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)}.

(4)

Properties of the error terms:

(a)

\rho_{N}(x,\eta)
are bounded away from
0,\infty
on
\mathfrak{S}_{1}\times[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
uniformly in
N
, and
\rho_{N}(x,\eta)\xrightarrow[\eta\to 0]{}1
uniformly in
N
and
x
.

(b)

For each
R>0
there exists
C=C_{R}>0
such that for all
\eta\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
and
N
,
C^{-1}|\eta|\leq|\xi_{N}(\eta)|\leq C|\eta|
and
\mathrm{sgn}(\xi(\eta))=\mathrm{sgn}(\eta)
.

The proof of this result will occupy us in §§
20.1
–
20.7
.

Theorem
19.5
above assumes irreducibility. Without this assumption we have a following weaker bound.

Theorem 19.6

Let
K:=\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{f}|
, and
suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
.
For each
{\varepsilon},R
there is
D({\varepsilon},R,K)
and
N_{0}
such that for all
z_{N}\in[\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}({\varepsilon}),b_{N}^{R}]
and
N>N_{0}
,

D^{-1}\leq\frac{\sqrt{V_{N}}{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}\geq z_{N})}{e^{-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)}}\leq D.

To assist the reader in digesting the statement of Theorem
19.5
, we now explain how to use it to obtain Theorems
19.3
,

19.4
on moderate deviations, as well as other consequences.

Proof of Theorems
19.3
and
19.4
:

By Theorem
19.5
(1),
\exists R>0
s.t.
if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
for all
N
large enough, and

\rho_{N}(x,\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1,\hskip 11.74988pt\xi_{N}(\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})\to 0,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{1}{b-a}\int_{a}^{b}e^{-t\xi_{N}(\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})}dt\to 1.

Suppose
G_{alg}(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})=\mathbb{R}
, then theorem
19.5
(2) implies that

{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]\sim\frac{|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp(-V_{N}\mathfs I_{N}(z_{N}/V_{N})).

Next, by Theorem
19.2
(2), if
\frac{z_{n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then

V_{N}\mathfs I_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)\sim\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{z_{n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\right)^{2},

whence
{\mathbb{P}}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]\sim\frac{|a-b|}{\sqrt{2\pi V_{N}}}\exp(-\frac{1+o(1)}{2}(\frac{z_{n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}})^{2})
. This proves Theorem
19.3
.
The proof of Theorem
19.4
is similar, and we leave it to the reader.
\square

Here are some other consequences of Theorem
19.5
.

Corollary 6.78

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain.
Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible, with algebraic range
\mathbb{R}
.

(1)

If
\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
then for any finite non empty interval
(a,b)
the distribution of

S_{N}-z_{N}
conditioned on
S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)
is asymptotically uniform on
(a,b).

(2)

If
\lim\inf\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}>0
and
there exists
R
s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
for all sufficiently large
N,

then the distribution of

\xi_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)\cdot(S_{N}-z_{N})\text{ conditioned on $S_{N}\geq z_{N}$}

is asymptotically exponential with parameter 1.

Remark.

The condition in (2) is satisfied whenever
\liminf\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}>0
, and
\limsup\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}>0
is small enough, see Theorem
19.5
(1).

Proof

To see part (1), note first that if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then

\xi_{N}=\xi_{N}(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})\to 0
, whence
\frac{1}{\beta-\alpha}\int_{\alpha}^{\beta}e^{-t\xi_{N}}dt\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1
for every non-empty interval
(\alpha,\beta)
.
Thus by Theorem
19.5
,
for every interval
[c,d]\subset[a,b]
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(c,d)]}{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]}=\frac{|c-d|}{|a-b|}.

(the prefactors
\rho_{N}
are identical, and they cancel out).

To see part (2), note first that our assumptions on
z_{N}
guarantee that

\xi_{N}=\xi_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)
is bounded
from away from zero and infinity, and that all its limit points are strictly positive.

Suppose
\xi_{N_{k}}\to\xi
. Then arguing as in part (1) it is not difficult to see that for all
(a,b)\subset(0,\infty)
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N_{k}}(S_{N_{k}}-z_{N_{k}})\in(a+r,b+r)|S_{N_{k}}>z_{N_{k}}]}{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N_{k}}(S_{N_{k}}-z_{N_{k}})\in(a,b)|S_{N_{k}}>z_{N_{k}}]}=e^{-r}.

Since this is true for all convergent
\{\xi_{N_{k}}\}
, and since any subsequence of
\{\xi_{N}\}
has a convergent subsequence,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a+r,b+r)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a,b)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}=e^{-r},

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a+r,b+r)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a,b)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}=e^{-r},

and so
\displaystyle{\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a+r,b+r)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}{{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})\in(a,b)|S_{N}>z_{N}]}=e^{-r}.}

So conditioned on
S_{N}>z_{N}
,
\xi_{N}(S_{N}-z_{N})
is asymptotically exponential with parameter
1
.
\square

Corollary 6.79

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain.
Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible, with algebraic range
\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
z_{N}
be a sequence of integers.

(1)

If
\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
then for any
a<b
in
\mathbb{Z}

the distribution of

S_{N}-z_{N}
conditioned on
S_{N}-z_{N}\in[a,b]
is asymptotically uniform on
[a,b].

(2)

If
\lim\inf\frac{z_{N}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}>0

and there exists
R
s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
for all sufficiently large
N,

\xi_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)\to\xi
,
then

(S_{N}-z_{N})\text{ conditioned on $S_{N}\geq z_{N}$}

is asymptotically geometric with parameter

e^{-\xi}.

The proof is similar to the proof in the non-lattice case, so we omit it.

It worthwhile to note the following consequence of this result. In the following statement, “local distribution” means a functional on
C_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and “vague convergence” means convergence on all continuous functions with compact support.

Corollary 6.80

Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain.
Let
z_{N}
be a sequence s.t.
for some
R
,
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]

for large
N.

Let
\zeta_{N}
be the local distribution of
S_{N}
around
z_{N},
that is

\zeta_{N}(\phi)={\mathbb{E}}_{x}(\phi(S_{N}-z_{N})).
Let
\zeta
be a vague limit of
\{q_{N}\zeta_{N}\}

for some
sequence
q_{N}>0.

If
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible then
\zeta
has density

c_{1}e^{c_{2}t}
with respect to the Haar measure on the algebraic
range of
\mathsf{f}
for some
c_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}.

If the restriction
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]

is dropped, then
it is likely that
\zeta
is either
as above, or an atomic measure with one atom, but our methods
are insufficient for proving this.

20
Proofs

We prove Theorems
19.1
,
19.2
,

19.5
and
19.6
. (Theorems
19.3
and
19.4
are direct consequences, and were proved in §
19.4
.)

We assume throughout that
\mathsf{\{}X_{n}\}
is a uniformly elliptic Markov chain with state spaces
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, transition probabilities
\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy)
, and stationary distributions
\mu_{k}(E):={\mathbb{P}}(X_{k}\in E)
. Let
\mathsf{f}=\{f_{n}\}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on
\mathsf{X}
. Let
\epsilon_{0}
denote the ellipticity constant of
\mathsf{X}
, and
K=\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{f}|
.

20.1
Strategy of proof

The proof can be briefly described as an implementation of ‘‘change of measure” technique (aka ‘‘Cramér’s transform”).

We explain the idea.
Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
, and let
z_{N}
be as in Theorem
19.5
.
We will modify the transition probabilities of
\mathsf{X}=\{X_{n}\}
to generate a Markov array
\widetilde{\mathsf{X}}=\{\widetilde{X}^{(N)}_{n}\}
whose row sums

\widetilde{S}_{N}={f}_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1}^{(N)},\widetilde{X}_{2}^{(N)})+\cdots+{f}_{N}(\widetilde{X}_{N}^{(N)},\widetilde{X}_{N+1}^{(N)})

satisfy

{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{S}_{N})}={o\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(\widetilde{S}_{N})}\right)}.

(20.1)

(
20.1
) places us in the regime of local deviations which we have analyzed in Chapter
4
. The results of that chapter provide asymptotics for
{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{S}_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
, and these can be translated into asymptotics for
{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
.

The array
\widetilde{\mathsf{X}}
is constructed from
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
as follows: Let
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
and
\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy)
denote the state spaces and transition probabilities of the original Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
, then we take
f^{(N)}_{n}=f_{n}
,
\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{n}=\mathfrak{S}_{n}
, and we let
\widetilde{\mathsf{X}}
be the Markov array with state spaces
\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{n}
and transition probabilities

{\widetilde{\pi}}_{n,n+1}^{(N)}(x,dy):=e^{\xi_{N}f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi_{N})}{e^{p_{n}(\xi_{N})}h_{n}(x,\xi_{N})}\cdot\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy).

Here
\xi_{N}
is a parameter that is calibrated to get (
20.1
), and
p_{n},h_{n},h_{n+1}
are chosen to guarantee that
{\widetilde{\pi}}_{n,n+1}^{(N)}(x,dy)
has total mass equal to one. This technique is called a “change of measure.”

The value of
\xi_{N}
depends on
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
. To construct
\xi_{N}
and to control it, we must know that
\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}
belong to a sets where
\mathfs F_{N}
are strictly convex, uniformly in
N
.
This is the reason why we need to assume that

\exists R
s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
for all
N
, a condition we can check as soon as

|\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}|<c
with
c
small enough.
9
9

9

Other situations where the condition

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
can be checked are discussed
in §
21
.

We remark that the dependence of
\xi_{N}
on
N
means that
\{\widetilde{X}^{(N)}_{n}\}
is an array, not a chain. The fact that the change of measure produces arrays from chains is the reason we insisted on working with arrays in the first part of this work.

20.2
A parameterized family of changes of measure

In this section
we construct, for an arbitrary given sequence of constants
\xi_{N}\in\mathbb{R}
, transition probabilities of the form

{\widetilde{\pi}}_{n,n+1}^{(N)}(x,dy):=e^{\xi_{N}f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi_{N})}{e^{p_{n}(\xi_{N})}h_{n}(x,\xi_{N})}\cdot\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy),

(20.2)

where
p_{n}(\xi_{N})
are real numbers and
h_{k}^{\xi_{N}}(\cdot)=h_{k}(\cdot,\xi_{N})
are positive functions on
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
