which are chosen to guarantee that
{\widetilde{\pi}}_{n,n+1}^{(N)}(x,dy)
has total mass equal to one.

We treat the sequence of parameters
\xi_{N}
as arbitrary.
In the next section we will explain how to choose a particular
\{\xi_{N}\}
to guarantee (
20.1
).

Lemma 6.81

Given
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
a sequence of real numbers
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
there are unique numbers
{p}_{n}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
, and unique non-negative
h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\in L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n},\mathfs B(\mathfrak{S}_{n}),\mu_{n})
s.t.

\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}h_{n}(x,\xi)\mu_{n}(dx)=\exp(a_{n}\xi)
for all
n
, and for a.e.
x

\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+1}}e^{\xi f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi)}{e^{p_{n}(\xi)}h_{n}(x,\xi)}\,\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy)=1.

(20.3)

The unique solution is positive almost everywhere.

Remark.:
Notice that if
\{\overline{h}_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\}
,
\{\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)\}
satisfy the Lemma with
a_{n}=0
, then the unique solution with general
\{a_{n}\}
is given by

h_{n}(\cdot,\xi):=e^{a_{n}\xi}\overline{h}_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\ ,\ p_{n}(\xi):=\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)-a_{n}\xi+a_{n+1}\xi.

(20.4)

Evidently,
h_{n},p_{n}
give rise to the same probability kernel (
20.2
) as do
\overline{h}_{n},\overline{p}_{n}
. We call
\{\overline{h}_{n}\}
and
\{\overline{p}_{n}\}
the
fundamental solution
.

Proof

It is enough to prove the existence and uniqueness of the fundamental solution, so henceforth we assume
a_{n}=0
. We may also assume without loss of generality that
|\xi|\leq 1
, else scale
\mathsf{f}
.

Set
V_{n}:=L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n},\mathfs B(\mathfrak{S}_{n}),\mu_{n})
, and define operators
L_{n}^{\xi}:V_{n+1}\to V_{n}
by

(L_{n}^{\xi}h)(x)=\int\limits_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+1}}e^{\xi f_{n}(x,y)}h(y)\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy).

(20.5)

The operators
L_{n}^{\xi}
are linear, bounded, and positive.

For (
20.3
) to hold, it is necessary and sufficient that
h_{n}^{\xi}(\cdot):=h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
be positive a.e., and
L_{n}^{\xi}h_{n+1}^{\xi}=e^{p_{n}(\xi)}h_{n}^{\xi}
for some
p_{n}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
.

Positivity everywhere may be replaced by the weaker property that
h_{n}^{\xi}\in L^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}
are all non-negative a.e., because for such functions, since
|\mathsf{f}|\leq K
a.s. and
\mathsf{X}
is uniformly elliptic with ellipticity constant
\epsilon_{0}
,

h_{n}^{\xi}(x)=e^{-p_{n}(\xi)-p_{n+1}(\xi)}(L_{n}^{\xi}L_{n+1}^{\xi}h_{n+2}^{\xi})(x)\geq e^{-p_{n}(\xi)-p_{n+1}(\xi)-2K}\epsilon_{0}\|h_{n+2}^{\xi}\|_{1}.

Thus to prove the lemma it is enough to find a sequence numbers
p_{n}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
and non-negative
h_{n}^{\xi}\in L^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}
such that
L_{n}^{\xi}h_{n+1}^{\xi}=e^{p_{n}(\xi)}h_{n}^{\xi}
for some
p_{n}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
.

The existence and uniqueness of such “generalized eigenvectors” can be proved as in

FS
,
BG
,
K
using
Hilbert’s projective metrics
. We recall what these are.
Let

C_{n}:=\{h\in V_{n}:h\geq 0\text{ a.e. }\}
. These are closed cones and

L_{n}^{\xi}(C_{n+1})\subset C_{n}.
Define

d_{n}(h,g):=\log\biggl(\frac{M(h|g)}{m(h|g)}\biggr)\in[0,\infty],\ \ (h,g\in C_{n}),

where
M=M(f|g),m=m(f|g)
are the best constants in the estimate
mh\leq f\leq Mh
. This is a pseudo-metric on the interior of
C_{n}
, and
d(h,g)=0\Leftrightarrow h,g
are proportional.
Also, for all
h,g\in C_{n}\setminus\{0\}
,

\left\|\frac{h}{\int h}-\frac{g}{\int g}\right\|_{1}\leq e^{d_{n}(h,g)}-1.

(20.6)

Birkhoff’s theorem
Bi
says that any linear map
T:C_{n+2}\to C_{n}
such that the
d_{n}
–diameter of
T(C_{n+2})
in
C_{n}
is less than some
\Delta>0
, contracts the Hilbert’s projective metric at least
by a factor
\theta:=\tanh(\Delta/4)\in(0,1)
.

We will apply Birkhoff’s theorem to the linear transformations

T_{n}^{\xi}:=L_{n}^{\xi}L_{n+1}^{\xi}:C_{n+2}\to C_{n}.

One checks using the standing assumptions and
|\xi|\leq 1
that

e^{-2K}\epsilon_{0}\|h\|_{1}\leq(T_{n}^{\xi}h)(x)\leq e^{2K}\epsilon_{0}^{-2}\|h\|_{1}\ \ \ (h\in C_{n+2}),

(20.7)

whence
d_{n}(T_{n}^{\xi}h,1)\leq 4K+3\log(1/\epsilon_{0})
. So the diameter of
T^{\xi}_{n}(C_{n+2})
in
C_{n}
is less than
\Delta:=8K+6\log(1/\epsilon_{0})
. Hence by Birkhoff’s Theorem mentioned above,

d_{n}(T_{n+1}^{\xi}h,T_{n+1}^{\xi}g)\leq\theta d_{n+2}(h,g)\ \ (h,g\in C_{n+2}).

(20.8)

where

\theta:=\tanh(2K+\frac{3}{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{0}))\in(0,1).

It follows that for every
n
,
\{L_{n}^{\xi}L_{n+1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{n+k-1}^{\xi}1_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+k}}\}_{k\geq 1}\subset C_{n}
is a Cauchy sequence with respect to
d_{n}
. By
(
20.6
),

\frac{L_{n}^{\xi}L_{n+1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{n+k-1}^{\xi}1_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+k}}}{\|L_{n}^{\xi}L_{n+1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{n+k-1}^{\xi}1_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+k}}\|_{1}}

is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{1}
.

The limiting function
h_{n}^{\xi}
has integral one, and is positive and bounded, because of (
20.7
).
Clearly,
L_{n}^{\xi}h_{n+1}^{\xi}=e^{p_{n}}h_{n}^{\xi}
for some
p_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
. So
\{h_{n}^{\xi}\},\{p_{n}\}
exist.

Moreover,
the proof shows that
{\rm diam}\left(\bigcap_{k\geq 1}L_{n}^{\xi}\cdots L_{n+k-1}^{\xi}(C_{n+k})\right)=0
. It follows that
h_{n}^{\xi}
is unique up to multiplicative constant, whence by the normalization condition, unique.
The lemma is proved.
\square

The proof has the following consequence, which
we mention for future reference:
For every
R>0
, there exists
C_{0}>0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
(depending on
R
)
such that for every
|\xi|\leq R

d_{1}\left(L_{1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{N}^{\xi}h_{N+1}^{\xi},\;L_{1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{N}^{\xi}1\right)\leq C_{0}\theta^{N/2}d_{N+1}\left(h_{N+1}^{\xi},1\right).

(20.9)

The case when
N
is even follows directly from (
20.8
) and does not require the constant
C_{0}
. The case of odd
N
is obtained from the even case by using the exponential contraction of
L_{2}^{\xi}\cdots L_{N}^{\xi}
and the fact that one additional application of
L_{1}^{\xi}
(or any other positive linear operator) does not increase the Hilbert norm. This implies (
20.9
) with
C_{0}:=\theta^{-1/2}
.

Lemma 6.82

Let
h_{n}^{\xi}(\cdot)=h(\cdot,\xi)
be as in Lemma
6.81
. If
a_{n}
is bounded, then for every
R>0
there is
C=C(R)
s.t. for all
n\geq 1
, a.e.
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
and
|\xi|\leq R
,

C^{-1}\leq h_{n}(x,\xi)\leq C\hskip 11.74988pt\text{and}\hskip 11.74988ptC^{-1}<e^{p_{n}(\xi)}<C.

Proof

It is enough to consider the fundamental solution (
a_{n}=0
,
\int h_{n}=1
); the general case follows from (
20.4
). It is also sufficient to consider the case
|\xi|\leq 1
; the general case follows by scaling
\mathsf{f}
.

Let
\{h_{n}^{\xi}\}
be the fundamental solution, then in the notation of the previous proof,
T^{\xi}_{n}h_{n+2}^{\xi}=e^{p_{n}(\xi)+p_{n+1}(\xi)}h_{n}^{\xi}
, whence by (
20.7
),

e^{-2K}\epsilon_{0}\leq e^{p_{n}(\xi)+p_{n+1}(\xi)}h_{n+2}^{\xi}\leq e^{2K}\epsilon_{0}^{-2}.

Integrating, and recalling that
\int h_{n+2}^{\xi}\,d\mu_{n+2}=\exp(a_{n+2}\xi)=1
, we obtain

e^{-2K}\epsilon_{0}\leq e^{p_{n}(\xi)+p_{n+1}(\xi)}\leq e^{2K}\epsilon_{0}^{-2}.

So
e^{-4K}\epsilon_{0}^{2}\leq h_{n}^{\xi}(\cdot)\leq e^{4K}\epsilon_{0}^{-4}
.

Observe that
e^{p_{n}}=\int L^{\xi}_{n}h_{n+1}^{\xi}d\mu_{n+1}=e^{\pm K}\int h_{n+1}^{\xi}d\mu_{n+1}
. So
e^{p_{n}}
is also uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity.
\square

In the next section we will choose
\xi_{N}
to guarantee (
20.1
), and as it turns out, the choice involves
a condition on
\frac{\partial p_{n}}{\partial\xi}
. Later, we will also require information on
\frac{\partial^{2}p_{n}}{\partial\xi^{2}}
. In preparation for this, we will now study the differentiability of

\xi\mapsto h_{n}^{\xi}\text{ and }\xi\mapsto p_{n}(\xi).

The map
\xi\mapsto h_{n}^{\xi}
takes values in the Banach space
L^{\infty}
. To analyze it, we will use the theory of
real-analytic maps into Banach spaces

Die
.

Let us briefly review this theory.
Suppose
\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{Y}
are Banach spaces. Let
a_{n}:\mathfrak{X}^{n}\to\mathfrak{Y}
be a multilinear map.
The
norm
of
a_{n}
is

\|a_{n}\|:=\sup\{\|a_{n}({x}_{1},\ldots,{x}_{n})\|:x_{i}\in\mathfrak{X},\ \|{x}_{i}\|\leq 1\text{ for all }i\}.

A multilinear map is called
symmetric
if it is invariant under the permutation of its coordinates.
Given
{x}\in\mathfrak{X}
, we denote

a_{n}{x}^{n}:=a_{n}({x},\ldots,{x}).

A
power series
is a formal expression
\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}{x}^{n}
where
a_{n}:\mathfrak{X}^{n}\to\mathfrak{Y}
are multilinear and symmetric.

A function
\phi:\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is called
real analytic
at
{x}_{0}
if there is some
r>0
and a power series
\sum a_{n}{x}^{n}
(called the
Taylor series at
x_{0}
)
such that
\sum\|a_{n}\|r^{n}<\infty
and

\phi(x)=\phi(x_{0})+\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}({x}-{x}_{0})^{n}

whenever
\|{x}-{x}_{0}\|<r
. One can check that if this happens, then

a_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\frac{1}{n!}\left.\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\right|_{t_{1}=0}\cdots\left.\frac{d}{dt_{n}}\right|_{t_{n}=0}\phi(x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}x_{i}).

(20.10)

Conversely, if
\sum a_{n}(x-x_{0})^{n}
has positive radius of convergence with
a_{n}
as in (
20.10
), then
\phi
is real-analytic, and equal to its Taylor series
\phi(x_{0})+\sum a_{n}(x-x_{0})^{n}
on a neighborhood of
x_{0}
.

Example 6.83

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{X}\times\mathfrak{X}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathfrak{X}
be the map

\phi(x,y,z):=x-y/z.
Then
\phi
is real-analytic at every
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})
such that
z_{0}\neq 0
, with Taylor series

\phi(x,y,z)=\phi(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}(x-x_{0},y-y_{0},z-z_{0})^{n},

where

\|a_{n}\|=O(\|y_{0}\|/|z_{0}|^{n+1})+O(n/|z_{0}|^{n+1})
.

Proof

If
|z-z_{0}|<|z_{0}|
, then
x-y/z=x-\frac{y}{z_{0}}\sum_{k\geq 0}(-1)^{k}\frac{1}{z_{0}^{k}}(z-z_{0})^{k}
. For each
n\geq 1
,
\underline{x}_{0}:=(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})
,
\underline{x}_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\ (1\leq i\leq n)
, and
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\phi(\underline{x}_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}\underline{x}_{i})=x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}x_{i}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{k+1}}{z_{0}^{k+1}}\left(y_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}y_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}z_{i}\right)^{k}

converges in norm whenever
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\in A_{n}:=\bigl[|\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}z_{i}|<|z_{0}|\bigr]
. In particular, on
A_{n}
, this series is real-analytic in each
t_{i}
, and can be differentiated term-by-term infinitely many times.

To find
a_{n}(\underline{x}_{1},\ldots,\underline{x}_{n})
we observe that the differential (
20.10
) is equal to the coefficient of
t_{1}\cdots t_{n}
in the previous series. So for
n>2
,

a_{n}(\underline{x}_{1},\ldots,\underline{x}_{n})=\frac{(-1)^{n+1}y_{0}}{z_{0}^{n+1}}\cdot z_{1}\cdots z_{n}+\frac{(-1)^{n}}{z_{0}^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}{z_{1}\cdots\widehat{z_{i}}\cdots z_{n}}

where the hat above
z_{i}
indicates that the
i
-th term should be omitted.
It follows that
\|a_{n}\|=O(\|y_{0}\|/|z_{0}|^{n+1})+O(n/|z_{0}|^{n})
.
\square

Lemma 6.84

The functions

\xi\mapsto h_{n}^{\xi},p_{n}(\xi)
are real-analytic. If
a_{n}
is bounded, then for every
R>0
there is
C(R)>0
s.t. for every
|\xi|\leq R
and
n\geq 1
,

\left\|\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\right\|_{\infty}\leq C(R),\hskip 11.74988pt\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\xi^{2}}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\right\|_{\infty}\leq C(R).

Proof

The proof is based on §3.3 in
Du
, although it is somewhat simpler because our setup is more elementary.

It is enough to consider the special case
R=1
and
a_{n}=0
. In particular,
\int h_{n}^{\xi}=1
.

Fix
|\xi|\leq 1
and let
T_{n}:=T_{n}^{\xi}
,
h_{n}(\cdot)=h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
be as in the proof of Lemma
6.81
.
Define two Banach spaces:

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle:=\left\{(S_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:\begin{array}[]{l}S_{n}:L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n+2})\to L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n})\text{ are bounded linear }\\
\text{operators, and }\|S\|:=\sup_{n}{\|S_{n}\|}<\infty\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle:=\{(\varphi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:\varphi_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n+2})\text{ , }\|\varphi\|:=\sup\|\varphi_{n}\|_{\infty}<\infty\}

Using (
20.7
), it is not difficult to see that
T:=(T_{n})
belongs to
X
. By Lemma
6.82
,
h:=(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
belongs to
Y
.

Step 1.

There exists
0<\delta<1
s.t. for every
(S,\varphi)\in X\times Y
, for all
|\xi|\leq 1
, if
\|S-T\|<\delta
and
\|\varphi-h\|<\delta
, then
\inf|\int(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})|>\delta
.

Proof.
By (
20.7
),
\|T_{n}\|\leq M
where
M:=e^{2K}\epsilon_{0}^{-2}
, and by Lemma
6.82
, there is a constant
{\varepsilon}_{1}>0
so that for all
n
and
|\xi|\leq 1

{\varepsilon}_{1}\leq(T_{n}h_{n+2})(x)\leq{\varepsilon}_{1}^{-1}.

So if
\|S-T\|<\delta
and
\|\varphi-h\|<\delta
, then for a.e.
x
,

\displaystyle S_{n}\varphi_{n+2}(x)

\displaystyle=(T_{n}h_{n+2})(x)-(T_{n}-S_{n})h_{n+2}(x)-S_{n}(h_{n+2}-\varphi_{n+2})(x)

\displaystyle\geq{\varepsilon}_{1}-\|T-S\|\|h\|-(\|S-T\|+\|T\|)\|h-\varphi\|

\displaystyle\geq{\varepsilon}_{1}-\delta\|h\|-(\delta+M)\delta.

Let
C
be a uniform upper bound for
\|h\|
which holds for all
|\xi|\leq 1
. If
0<\delta<(\frac{{\varepsilon}_{1}}{C+M+2})\wedge 1
, then
S_{n}\varphi_{n+2}>\delta
a.e., and the step follows.

Henceforth we fix
\delta
as in step 1. Let
B_{\delta}(T):=\{S\in X:\|S-T\|<\delta\}
and
B_{\delta}(h):=\{\varphi\in Y:\|\varphi-h\|<\delta\}
, and define

\Upsilon:B_{\delta}(T)\times B_{\delta}(h)\to Y,\ \hskip 11.74988pt\Upsilon(S,\varphi):=\left(\varphi_{n}-\frac{S_{n}\varphi_{n+2}}{\int(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})d\mu_{n+2}}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}.

This is well-defined by the choice of
\delta
, and
\Upsilon(T,h)=0
.

Step 2.

\Upsilon
is real-analytic on
B_{\delta}(T)\times B_{\delta}(h)
.

Proof.
First we write
\Upsilon=\Phi(\Upsilon^{(1)},\Upsilon^{(2)},\Upsilon^{(3)})
with

\circ

\Upsilon^{(1)}:X\times Y\to Y
,
\Upsilon^{(1)}(S,\varphi)=\varphi

\circ

\Upsilon^{(2)}:X\times Y\to Y
,
\Upsilon^{(2)}(S,\varphi)=(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

\circ

\Upsilon^{(3)}:X\times Y\to\ell^{\infty}
,
\Upsilon^{(3)}(S,\varphi)=(\int(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})d\mu_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

\circ

\Phi:\{(\varphi,\psi,\xi)\in Y\times Y\times\ell^{\infty}:\inf|\xi_{i}|>0\}\to Y
,

\Phi((\varphi,\psi,\xi)_{i\geq 1})=(\varphi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{-1}\psi_{i})_{i\geq 1}.

By step 1,
\overset{\to}{\Upsilon}:=(\Upsilon^{(1)},\Upsilon^{(2)},\Upsilon^{(3)})
maps
B_{\delta}(T)\times B_{\delta}(h)
into

U:=\{(\varphi,\psi,\xi)\in Y\times Y\times\ell^{\infty}:\|\varphi\|<C+\delta,\|\psi\|<M+\delta,\ \inf|\xi_{i}|>\delta/2\},

whence into
the domain of
\Phi
.

We claim that for each of the functions
\Upsilon^{(i)}
, some high enough derivative of
\Upsilon^{(i)}
is identically zero. Let
D
be the derivative, and let
D_{i}
be the partial derivative with respect to the
i
-th variable, then

(1)

\Upsilon^{(1)}
is linear, so
(D\Upsilon^{(1)})(S,\varphi)
is constant, and
D^{2}\Upsilon^{(1)}=0
.

(2)

\Upsilon^{(2)}:X\times Y\to Y
,
\Upsilon^{(2)}(S,\varphi)=(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. Here

\begin{array}[]{l}(D_{1}\Upsilon^{(2)})(S,\varphi)(S^{\prime})=(S^{\prime}_{n}\varphi_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\hskip 28.45274pt,\hskip 28.45274pt(D_{1}^{2}\Upsilon^{(2)})(S,\varphi)=0\\
(D_{2}\Upsilon^{(2)})(S,\varphi)(\varphi^{\prime})=(S_{n}\varphi^{\prime}_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\hskip 28.45274pt,\hskip 28.45274pt(D_{2}^{2}\Upsilon^{(2)})(S,\varphi)=0\\
(D_{1}D_{2}\Upsilon^{(2)})(S,\varphi)(S^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})=(S_{n}^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\end{array}

We see that
D^{2}\Upsilon^{(2)}
does not depend on
(S,\varphi)
, so
D^{3}\Upsilon=0

(3)

\Upsilon^{(3)}:X\times Y\to\ell^{\infty}
,
\Upsilon^{(3)}(S,\varphi)=(\int(S_{n}\varphi_{n+2})d\mu_{n+2})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. As before, the third derivative is zero.

Consequently,
\Upsilon^{(i)}
are real-analytic on its domain (with finite Taylor series at every point).
Next we show that
\Phi
is real-analytic on
U
.
To do this we recall that by Example
6.83
,

\displaystyle x-\frac{y}{z}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})(x-x_{0},y-y_{0},z-z_{0})^{n}

where
a_{n}(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0}):(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
are symmetric multilinear functions depending on
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})
, s.t.

\|a_{n}(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})\|=O(|y_{0}|/|z_{0}|^{n+1})+O(n/|z_{0}|^{n})
.
So

\Phi(\varphi,\psi,\xi)=\Phi(\varphi^{(0)},\psi^{(0)},\xi^{(0)})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}A_{n}(\varphi-\varphi^{(0)},\psi-\psi^{(0)},\xi-\xi^{(0)})^{n},

(20.11)

where
A_{n}:(Y\times Y\times\ell^{\infty})^{n}\to Y
, has entries

\displaystyle A_{n}((\varphi^{(1)},\psi^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}),\ldots,(\varphi^{(n)},\psi^{(n)},\xi^{(n)}))_{i}(x):=

\displaystyle a_{n}\bigl(\varphi^{(0)}_{i}(x),\psi^{(0)}_{i}(x),\xi^{(0)}_{i}\bigr)((\varphi_{i}^{(1)}(x),\psi_{i}^{(1)}(x),\xi^{(1)}_{i}),\ldots,(\varphi_{i}^{(n)}(x),\psi_{i}^{(n)}(x),\xi^{(n)}_{i}))

A_{n}
inherits multilinearity and symmetry from
a_{n}
, and
by construction,

\displaystyle\|A_{n}(\varphi^{(0)},\psi^{(0)},\xi^{(0)}))\|\leq

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\|a_{n}(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})\|:|x_{0}|,|y_{0}|\leq C+M+\delta,|z_{0}|>\frac{\delta}{2}\right\}=O(2^{n}n/\delta^{n}).

So the right-hand-side of (
20.11
) has positive radius of convergence, proving the analyticity of
\Phi:U\to Y
.

The step follows from the well-known result that the composition of real-analytic functions is real-analytic, see
Die
.

Claim 4.

(D_{2}\Upsilon)(T,h):Y\to Y
, the partial derivative of
\Upsilon
at
(T,h)
with respect to the second variable, has bounded inverse.

Proof.
A direct calculation shows that
(D_{2}\Upsilon)(T,h)(\varphi)=\varphi-\Lambda\varphi
, where

(\Lambda\varphi)_{n}=\frac{T_{n}\varphi_{n+2}}{\int(T_{n}h_{n+2})d\mu_{n}}-\left(\frac{\int(T_{n}\varphi_{n+2})d\mu_{n}}{\int(T_{n}h_{n+2})d\mu_{n}}\right)h_{n}.

To prove the claim, we show that
\Lambda
has spectral radius
<1
.

Let
T^{(k)}_{n}:=T_{n}T_{n+2}\cdots T_{n+2(k-1)}
, then we claim that

(\Lambda^{k}\varphi)_{n}=\frac{T^{(k)}_{n}\varphi_{n+2k}}{\int(T^{(k)}_{n}h_{n+2k})d\mu_{n}}-\left(\frac{\int(T_{n}^{(k)}\varphi_{n+2k})d\mu_{n}}{\int(T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k})d\mu_{n}}\right)h_{n}.

(20.12)

To see this we first note, using
T_{m}h_{m+2}\propto h_{m}
and
\int h_{m}d\mu_{m}=1
, that

\int(T^{(k+1)}_{n}h_{n+2(k+1)})d\mu_{n}=\int(T_{n}h_{n+2})d\mu_{n}\int(T^{(k)}_{n+2}h_{n+2(k+1)})d\mu_{n+2}.

With this identity in mind, the formula for
\Lambda^{k}
follows by induction.

We now explain why (
20.12
) implies that the spectral radius of
\Lambda
is less than one.
Fix
\varphi\in Y
. Recall that
C^{-1}\leq h_{n}\leq C
for all
n
, and let

\psi:=\varphi+{2C\|\varphi\|}h.

Then
\psi\in Y
,
\Lambda^{k}\psi=\Lambda^{k}\varphi
for all
k
(because
\Lambda h=0
), and for all
n

C\|\varphi\|h_{n}\leq\psi_{n}\leq 3C\|\varphi\|h_{n}

(20.13)

In particular, if
C_{n}
is the cone from the proof of Lemma
6.81
, and
d_{n}
is its projective Hilbert metric, then
\psi_{n}\in C_{n}
and
d_{n}(\psi_{n},h_{n})\leq\log 3
.
Since
T_{n}
contracts the Hilbert projective norm by a factor
\theta\in(0,1)
,

d_{n}(T^{(k)}_{n}\psi_{n+2k},T^{(k)}_{n}h_{n+2k})\leq\theta^{k}\log 3.

This implies by the definition of
d_{n}
that for a.e.
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
,

\left|\frac{(T^{(k)}_{n}\psi_{n+2k})(x)/\int(T^{(k)}_{n}\psi_{n+2k})}{(T^{(k)}_{n}h_{n+2k})(x)/\int(T^{(k)}_{n}h_{n+2k})}-1\right|\leq\max\{3^{\theta^{k}}-1,1-3^{-\theta^{k}}\}=3^{\theta^{k}}-1=:{\varepsilon}_{k}.

The denominator simplifies to
h_{n}
. So

\left\|\frac{(T^{(k)}_{n}\psi_{n+2k})}{\int(T^{(k)}_{n}\psi_{n+2k})}-h_{n}\right\|_{\infty}\leq{\varepsilon}_{k}\|h\|.

(20.14)

Next we use the positivity of
T_{n}^{(k)}
and (
20.13
) to note that

C\|\varphi\|T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k}\leq T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}\leq 3C\|\varphi\|T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k}.

We deduce that

C\|\varphi\|\leq\frac{\int(T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k})}{\int(T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k})}\leq 3C\|\varphi\|.

(20.15)

By (
20.12
), (
20.14
) and (
20.15
),

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{k}\varphi\|_{\infty}\equiv\|\Lambda^{k}\psi\|=\sup_{n}\left\|\frac{T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}}{\int T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k}}-\frac{\int T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}}{\int T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k}}\cdot h_{n}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n}\left\|\frac{T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}}{\int T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}}-h_{n}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\sup_{n}\left\|\frac{\int T_{n}^{(k)}\psi_{n+2k}}{\int T_{n}^{(k)}h_{n+2k}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 3C\epsilon_{k}\|h\|\cdot\|\varphi\|,

whence
\rho(\Lambda)\leq\lim\sqrt[k]{\epsilon_{k}}=\theta<1
.

Completion of the proof of the Lemma.
We constructed a real-analytic function
\Upsilon:X\times Y\to Y
such that
\Upsilon(T,h)=0
and
(D_{2}\Upsilon)(T,h):Y\to Y
has a bounded inverse.
By the implicit function theorem for real-analytic functions on Banach spaces
Wh
,
T
has a neighborhood
W\subset X
where one can define a real-analytic function
h:W\to Y
so that
\Upsilon(S,h(S))=0
.

Recall that
T=T^{\xi}:=\{T_{n}^{\xi}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
h=\{h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\}_{n\geq 1}
.
By the uniqueness part of Lemma
6.81
,
h(T)=h(\cdot,\xi)
. It is easy to see using
\mathrm{ess\,}\sup|\mathsf{f}|<\infty
that
\xi\mapsto T^{\xi}
is real-analytic (even holomorphic). So

\xi\mapsto h(T^{\xi})
is real-analytic, whence continuously differentiable infinitely many times.
Thus
\xi\mapsto h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
is real-analytic for all
n
, and

\left\{\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial\xi^{k}}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\right\}_{n\geq 1}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi^{k}}h(T^{\xi})\in Y

for all
k
.
By the definition of
Y
,
\sup\limits_{|\xi|\leq 1}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\|\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{\infty}=\|\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi}h(T)\|<\infty
and

\sup\limits_{|\xi|\leq 1}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\xi^{2}}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{\infty}=\|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\xi^{2}}h(T)\|<\infty
.

\square

20.3
Choosing the parameters

Given
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
\{a_{n}\}\subset\mathbb{R}
bounded, let
\{\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
denote the Markov chain with the initial distribution and state spaces of
\mathsf{X}
, but with transition probabilities

\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n+1}^{\xi}(x,dy)=e^{\xi f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi)}{e^{p_{n}(\xi)}h_{n}(x,\xi)}\cdot\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy),

where
p_{n}(\xi)
and
h_{k}^{\xi}(\cdot)=h_{k}(\cdot,\xi)
are as in Lemma
6.81
.
(This chain does not depend on the choice of
\{a_{n}\}
, see the remark after the statement of Lemma
6.81
.)
Denote the expectation and variance operators of this chain by
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}
,
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}
.

In this section we show that if
V_{N}:={\rm Var}(S_{N})\to\infty
and
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}

is sufficiently small, then it is possible to choose
\xi_{N}
and
a_{n}
bounded s.t.

\frac{z_{N}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{\widetilde{V}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})}}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0\hskip 11.74988pt\text{and}\hskip 11.74988pt\sum_{n=1}^{N}p_{n}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{N}).

Indeed, we will find
\xi_{N}
so that
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})=z_{N}+O(1)
.
The construction will show that if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then
\xi_{N}\to 0
.

Let
\overline{h}_{n}^{\xi}:=\overline{h}_{n}(\cdot,\xi):\mathfrak{S}_{n}\to(0,\infty)
and
\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
be the fundamental solution:
L_{n}^{\xi}\overline{h}_{n+1}^{\xi}=e^{\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)}\overline{h}_{n}^{\xi}
and
\int\overline{h}_{n}(x,\xi)\mu_{n}(dx)=1
. Then
\overline{h}_{n}^{\xi}=e^{-a_{n}\xi}h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
and
\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)=p_{n}(\xi)+a_{n}\xi-a_{n+1}\xi
so

\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n+1}^{\xi}(x,dy)=e^{\xi f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{\overline{h}_{n+1}(x,\xi)}{e^{\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)}\overline{h}_{n}(y,\xi)}\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy).

Let

\overline{P}_{N}(\xi):=\overline{p}_{1}(\xi)+\cdots+\overline{p}_{N}(\xi).

Lemma 6.85

\xi\mapsto\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)
is real analytic, and for every
R>0
there is a constant
C(R)
such that for all
|\xi|\leq R
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(1)

|\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})|\leq C(R)
;

(2)

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
.
Then
C(R)^{-1}\leq\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})/V_{N}\leq C(R)
for all
N
and
|\xi|\leq R
,
and

\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)/\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1\text{ uniformly in $|\xi|\leq R$.}

Proof

We have the identity
e^{\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)}=\int(L_{1}^{\xi}\cdots L_{N}^{\xi}\overline{h}_{N+1}^{\xi})(x)\mu_{1}(dx)
. Since

\xi\mapsto\overline{h}^{\xi}
and
\xi\mapsto L_{n}^{\xi}
are real-analytic,
\xi\mapsto\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)
is real-analytic.

Given
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
(the state space of
X_{1}
), define two measures on
\prod_{i=2}^{N+1}\mathfrak{S}_{i}
so that for every
E_{i}\in\mathfs B(\mathfrak{S}_{i})
(
1\leq i\leq N+1
),

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(E_{2}\times\cdots\times E_{N+1})

\displaystyle:={\mathbb{P}}(X_{2}\in E_{2},\ldots,X_{N+1}\in E_{N+1}|X_{1}=x_{1}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}^{\xi}_{x}(E_{2}\times\cdots\times E_{N+1})

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}^{\xi}(\widetilde{X}_{2}^{\xi}\in E_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{N+1}\in E_{N+1}|\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{1}=x_{1}).

Let
S_{N}(x,\underline{y}):=f(x,y_{1})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(y_{i},y_{i+1})
, then

\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}(y_{2},\ldots,y_{N+1})=e^{\xi S_{N}(x,\underline{y})}e^{-\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)}\left(\frac{\overline{h}_{N+1}(y_{N+1},\xi)}{\overline{h}_{1}(x,\xi)}\right).

By Lemma
6.84
,
\xi\mapsto\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}(y_{2},\ldots,y_{N+1})
is real-analytic. Differentiating, gives

\frac{d}{d\xi}\bigl[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\bigr]=\left[S_{N}(x,\underline{y})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)\frac{\overline{h}_{1}(x,\xi)}{\overline{h}_{N+1}(y_{N+1},\xi)}\frac{d}{d\xi}\left(\frac{\overline{h}_{N+1}(y_{N+1},\xi)}{\overline{h}_{1}(x,\xi)}\right)\right]\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}.

We write this as

\frac{d}{d\xi}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]=\left[S_{N}(x,\underline{y})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+\epsilon_{N}(x,y_{N+1},\xi)\right]\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}},

(20.16)

where
\epsilon_{N}(x,y_{N+1},\xi):=\frac{\overline{h}_{1}(x,\xi)}{\overline{h}_{N+1}(y_{N+1},\xi)}\frac{d}{d\xi}\left(\frac{\overline{h}_{N+1}(y_{N+1},\xi)}{\overline{h}_{1}(x,\xi)}\right)
.
By Lemmas
6.82
and
6.84
,

\epsilon_{N}(x,y_{N+1},\xi)
is
uniformly bounded in
N
,
x,\underline{y}
, and
|\xi|\leq R
.

By the intermediate value theorem and the uniform boundedness of
\xi\mapsto\frac{d}{d\xi}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]
on compact subsets of
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,

\frac{1}{\delta}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi+\delta}}{d\pi_{x}}-\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]

is uniformly bounded for
0<|h|<1
. So by the bounded convergence theorem

\int\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{h}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi+\delta}}{d\pi_{x}}-\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]d\pi_{x}=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\int\frac{1}{h}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi+\delta}}{d\pi_{x}}-\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]d\pi_{x}=0.

So
\int\frac{d}{d\xi}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}^{\xi}_{x}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]d\pi_{x}=0
, whence by (
20.16
),

0=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}_{x}(S_{N})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+O(1),

where
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}_{x}=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(\cdot|\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{1}=x)
.
Integrating with respect to
x
we obtain that

\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})+O(1)

uniformly in
|\xi|\leq R
,
N\to\infty
.

Differentiating (
20.16
) again we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{d\xi^{2}}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\right]=\frac{d}{d\xi}\left[\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}\left(S_{N}(x,\underline{y})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+\epsilon_{N}(x,y_{N+1},\xi)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}}{d\pi_{x}}\left[\left(S_{N}(x,\underline{y})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+\epsilon_{N}(x,y_{N+1},\xi)\right)^{2}-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+\frac{d\epsilon_{N}}{d\xi}\right].

By Lemmas
6.82
and
6.84
,
\frac{d\epsilon_{N}}{d\xi}
is uniformly bounded in
x,y_{N+1},N
and
|\xi|\leq R
.
As before,
\int\frac{d^{2}}{d\xi^{2}}\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}d\pi_{x}=\frac{d^{2}}{d\xi^{2}}\int\frac{d\widetilde{\pi}_{x}^{\xi}}{d\pi_{x}}d\pi_{x}=0
, whence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left[\left(S_{N}-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+O(1)\right)^{2}\right]-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+O(1)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left[\left(S_{N}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})+O(1)\right)^{2}\right]-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+O(1),

(20.17)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})-\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+O\left(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})}\right)

where the
O(1)
terms are uniformly bounded in
N
when
|\xi|\leq R
.

If
|\xi|\leq R
, then
\widetilde{\pi}^{\xi}_{n,n+1}(x,dy)
are uniformly elliptic with
{\varepsilon}_{0}
replaced by
{\varepsilon}_{0}/(C^{2}e^{KR})
for the
C
in Lemma
6.82
. Therefore by Theorem
2.30
,
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})\asymp\sum_{n=3}^{N}u_{n}^{2}(\xi)
where
u_{n}(\xi)
are the structure constants of
\{\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{n}\}
. Clearly,
u_{n}(\xi)\asymp u_{n}
where
u_{n}=u_{n}(0)
are the structure constants of
\{X_{n}\}
. So
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})\asymp V_{N}\to\infty
where the multiplicative error bounds is uniform in
N
and
|\xi|\leq R
.
By (
20.17
),

\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)/\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1
.
\square

The choice of
a_{N}
:
Lemma
6.85
(1) with
\xi=0
says that
\overline{P}_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{N})+O(1)
.
The error term is a nuisance, and we will choose
a_{n}
to get rid of it. Given
N
, let

a_{n}:={\mathbb{E}}(S_{n-1})-\overline{P}^{\prime}_{n-1}(0)\ ,\ a_{1}:=0\\

(20.18)

This is a bounded sequence, because of Lemma
6.85
(1).
The choice of
\{a_{n}\}
leads to the following objects:

\displaystyle h_{n}^{\xi}(x)=h_{n}(x,\xi):=\exp(a_{n}\xi)\overline{h}_{n}(x,\xi),

(20.19)

\displaystyle p_{n}(\xi):=\overline{p}_{n}(\xi)+(a_{n+1}-a_{n})\xi.

The transition kernel
\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n+1}^{\xi}
is left unchanged, because the differences between
\overline{h}_{n}
and
h_{n}
and between
\overline{p}_{n}
and
p_{n}
cancel out. But now,

P_{N}(\xi):=p_{1}(\xi)+\cdots+p_{N}(\xi)\equiv\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)+\bigl({\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})-\overline{P}^{\prime}_{N}(0)\bigr)\xi,

(20.20)

satisfies
P_{N}^{\prime}(0)={\mathbb{E}}(S_{N})
.

Properties of
P_{N}(\xi)
:

These functions turn out to be closely related to the distributional properties of
\mathsf{X}
and its change of measure
\mathsf{X}^{\xi}
.

Recall that
\mathfs F_{N}(\xi):=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})
, and that
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}
is the variance of
S_{N}
with respect to the change of measure
\widetilde{X}^{\xi}
. Then:

Lemma 6.86

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty

then
\xi\mapsto{P}_{N}(\xi)
is real analytic, and

(1)

P_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{N})

(2)

For every
R>0
, there exists
C(R)>0
s.t.

|{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})|\leq C(R)\ \ \text{ for all\ $|\xi|\leq R,N\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(3)

For every
R>0
, there exists
C(R)>0
s.t.

C(R)^{-1}\leq\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})/V_{N}\leq C(R)\ \ \text{ for all\ $|\xi|\leq R,\;N\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(4)

{P}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)/\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(S_{N})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}1
uniformly on compact subsets of
\xi
.

(5)

P_{N}(\xi)/V_{N}=\mathfs F_{N}(\xi)+o(V_{N}^{-1})
uniformly on compact subsets of
\xi
, as
N\to\infty
. Specifically,
let

\Delta_{N}(R):=\sup\limits_{|\xi|\leq R}V_{N}\left|\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)-\frac{P_{N}(\xi)}{V_{N}}\right|.
Then

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{N}\Delta_{N}(R)<\infty
for all
R>0
, and
\sup\limits_{N}\Delta_{N}(R)\xrightarrow[R\to 0^{+}]{}0.

(6)

P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)/V_{N}=\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)+O(V_{N}^{-1})
uniformly on compact subsets of
\xi
, as
N\to\infty
. Specifically,
let

{\overline{\Delta}}_{N}(R):=\sup\limits_{|\xi|\leq R}V_{N}\left|\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)-\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)}{V_{N}}\right|.
Then

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{N\geq N_{0}}{\overline{\Delta}}_{N}(R)<\infty.

Proof

The real analyticity of
P_{N}(\xi)
and parts (1)–(4) follow directly from Lemma
6.85
, the identity

P_{N}(\xi)=\overline{P}_{N}(\xi)+(a_{N+1}-a_{1})\xi
, and the boundedness of
a_{n}
.

The proof of part (5) uses the operators
L_{n}^{\xi}:L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n+1})\to L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n})
from (
20.5
),

(L_{n}^{\xi}h)(x):=\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{n+1}}e^{\xi f_{n}(x,y)}h(y)\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy)\equiv\mathbb{E}_{x}[e^{\xi f_{n}(x,X_{n+1})}h(X_{n+1})].

Let
h_{n}^{\xi}:=h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)\in L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{n})
be the unique positive functions constructed so that
L_{n}^{\xi}h^{\xi}_{n+1}=e^{p_{n}(\xi)}h_{n}^{\xi},\text{ where $p_{1}(\xi)+\cdots+p_{N}(\xi)=P_{N}(\xi)$.}

(To construct
h_{n}^{\xi}
, apply Lemma
6.81
with
a_{n}
as in
(
20.19
).)
In particular,
h_{n}^{0}\equiv 1
and

{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(e^{\xi S_{N}}h_{N+1}(X_{N+1})\right)=e^{P_{N}(\xi)}h_{1}^{\xi}(x).

(20.21)

By Lemma
6.82
, there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(R)>1
such that
C_{1}^{-1}\leq h_{N+1}^{\xi}\leq C_{1}
for all
|\xi|\leq R
and
N\geq 1
. Thus by
(
20.21
),

C_{1}(R)^{-2}e^{P_{N}(\xi)}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{\xi S_{N}}\right)\leq C_{1}(R)^{2}e^{P_{N}(\xi)}.

Taking logarithms, we deduce that
|\mathfs F_{N}(\xi)-P_{N}(\xi)/V_{N}|\leq 2C_{1}(R)/V_{N}
for all
N\geq 1
and
|\xi|\leq R
. Equivalently,
\sup_{N}\Delta_{N}(R)\leq 2C_{1}
.

Next, by Lemma
6.84
and the identity
h_{n}^{0}\equiv 1
,
\|h_{N}^{\xi}-1\|_{\infty}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0
uniformly on compact subsets of
\xi
. Returning to the definition of
C_{1}(R)
we find that we may choose
C_{1}(R)\xrightarrow[R\to 0^{+}]{}1
. As before, this implies that
\sup_{N}\Delta_{N}(R)\xrightarrow[R\to 0]{}0
.

Here is the proof of part (6). Fix
R>0
and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}
denote the expectation operator with respect to the change of measure
\mathsf{X}^{\xi}
, then

V_{N}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(S_{N}e^{\xi S_{N}})}{{\mathbb{E}}(e^{\xi S_{N}})}=\frac{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N}(h_{1}^{\xi}/h_{N+1}^{\xi}))}{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(h_{1}^{\xi}/h_{N+1}^{\xi})}.

(20.22)

We have already remarked that
\mathsf{X}^{\xi}
are uniformly elliptic, and that their uniform ellipticity constants are bounded away from zero for
\xi
ranging on a compact set. This gives us the mixing bounds in Proposition
1.11
with the same
C_{mix}>0
,
0<\theta<1
for all
|\xi|\leq R
. So

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left(\frac{h_{1}^{\xi}S_{N}}{h_{N+1}^{\xi}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(h_{1}^{\xi})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left(1/h_{N+1}^{\xi}\right)\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})+O(1)\text{ as $N\to\infty$},

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left(\frac{h_{1}^{\xi}}{h_{N+1}^{\xi}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(h_{1}^{\xi})\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left(1/h_{N+1}^{\xi}\right)+O(\theta^{N}),\text{ as $N\to\infty$}

where the big oh’s are uniform for
|\xi|\leq R
. Plugging this into (
20.22
) gives

V_{N}\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(S_{N})+O(1)\text{ as $N\to\infty$, uniformly for $|\xi|\leq R$}.

Part (6) follows from this from part (2) of the lemma.

\square

The choice of
\xi_{N}
:
We choose
\xi_{N}
so that

{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}\ ,\ \widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})=z_{N}+O(1).

The following lemma gives sufficient conditions for the existence of such
\xi_{N}
.

Lemma 6.87

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
,
R>0
, and

[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]:=\left[\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-R)-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}},\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(R)-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right].

(1)

For each
R
there is
C(R)
,
N(R)
s.t. if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
, and
N>N(R)
then

(a)

\exists!\xi_{N}\in[-(R+1),(R+1)]
s.t.
{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}
;

(b)

C(R)^{-1}\left|\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right|\leq|\xi_{N}|\leq C(R)\left|\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right|
;

(c)

\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{N})=\mathrm{sgn}(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})
;

(d)

\left|\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})-z_{N}\right|\leq C(R)
.

(2)

For every
R>1

there exists
c(R)>0
such that for all
N
large enough,

\text{if $\left|\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right|\leq c(R)$, then $\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]$}.

(20.23)

Consequently, if
|\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}|<c(R)
, then there exists a unique
\xi_{N}
with (a)–(d) above.

Proof

Let

\displaystyle[{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R}]:=\left[\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(-R)-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}},\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(R)-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right].

Claim:

For all
R>0
, for all
N
large enough,

[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]\subset[{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+1},{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+1}]\subset[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R+2},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R+2}].

Proof of the claim:
By parts (3) and (4) of Lemma
6.86
, there exists
\delta>0
such that
P_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)/V_{N}\geq\delta
on
[-(R+2),(R+2)]
. Thus by the mean value theorem,

{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+2}\geq{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+1}+\delta,\ \ {\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+1}\geq{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R}+\delta,\ \ {\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+2}\leq{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+1}-\delta,\ \ {\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+1}\leq{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R}-\delta.

Next by part (6) of Lemma
6.86
,

|{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R^{\prime}}-{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R^{\prime}}|=O(V_{N}^{-1})
and
|{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R^{\prime}}-{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R^{\prime}}|=O(V_{N}^{-1})
for all
R^{\prime}\leq R+2.

For all
N
large enough
|O(V_{N}^{-1})|<\delta
, and

{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R+2}<{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+1}<{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R}<{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}<{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+1}<{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R+2},

which proves the claim.

We can now prove part (1) of the lemma. Let

\displaystyle\varphi_{N}(\xi):=\frac{{P}_{N}(\xi)-\xi{P}_{N}^{\prime}(0)}{V_{N}}.

By Lemma
6.86
,
\varphi_{N}(\xi)
is strictly convex, smooth, and

{P}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{iff}\hskip 11.74988pt\varphi_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=\frac{z_{N}-{P}_{N}^{\prime}(0)}{V_{N}}.

Fix
R>0
. By the claim, for all
N
large enough, if
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
, then

\frac{z_{N}-P_{N}^{\prime}(0)}{V_{N}}\equiv\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R+1},{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R+1}]\equiv\varphi_{N}^{\prime}[-(R+1),(R+1)].

Since
\varphi_{N}^{\prime}
is continuous and strictly increasing, there
\exists!\xi_{N}\in[-(R+1),(R+1)]
such that
\varphi_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=\frac{z_{N}-P_{N}^{\prime}(0)}{V_{N}}
. Equivalently, there exists a unique
|\xi_{N}|\leq R+1
such that
P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}
.

This argument shows that for every
N
sufficiently large, for every
\eta\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]

there exists a unique
\xi=\xi(\eta)\in[-(R+1),(R+1)]
such that

\varphi_{N}^{\prime}(\xi(\eta))=\eta.

By Lemma
6.86
,
\exists\delta(R)>0
so that
\delta(R)\leq\varphi_{N}^{\prime\prime}\leq\delta(R)^{-1}
on
[-(R+1),(R+1)]
. So
\eta\mapsto\xi(\eta)
is

\frac{1}{\delta(R)}
-bi-Lipschitz on
[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
.
By construction,
\varphi_{N}^{\prime}(0)=0
. So
\xi(0)=0
, whence by the bi-Lipschitz property

\delta(R)|\eta|\leq|\xi(\eta)|\leq\delta(R)^{-1}|\eta|\text{ on }[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}].

Since
\varphi_{N}
is real-analytic and strictly convex,
\varphi_{N}^{\prime}
is smooth and strictly increasing. By the inverse mapping theorem,
\eta\mapsto\xi(\eta)
is smooth and strictly increasing. So

\mathrm{sgn}(\xi(\eta))=\mathrm{sgn}(\eta)\text{ on }[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}].

Specializing to the case
\eta=\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
, gives properties (a)–(c) of
\xi_{N}
.

Property (d) is because of by Lemma
6.86
, which says that

z_{N}=P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=\widetilde{E}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})+O(1).

Notice that the big oh is uniform because
|\xi_{N}|\leq R+1
. This completes the proof of part (1).

Here is the proof of part (2): For every
R>1
, for all
N
large enough

\displaystyle[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]\supset[{\widetilde{a}}_{N}^{R-1},{\widetilde{b}}_{N}^{R-1}]\equiv\varphi_{N}^{\prime}[-(R-1),(R-1)]\text{ ($\because$ claim, $\varphi_{N}^{\prime}$ is increasing)}

\displaystyle\supset[-\delta(R-1)(R-1),\delta(R-1)(R-1)]\ \ (\because\varphi_{N}^{\prime}(0)=0,\varphi_{N}^{\prime\prime}\geq\delta(R+1)).

So
[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]\supset[-c,c]

for
R\geq 2
where
c:=\delta(1)
.

\square

Corollary 6.88

Suppose
V_{N}\to\infty
and
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then for all
N
large enough, there exists a unique
\xi_{N}
such that
P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}
. Furthermore,
\xi_{N}\to 0
.

20.4
The asymptotic behavior of
\widetilde{V}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})

Let
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}
denote the variance of
S_{N}
with respect to the change of measure
\mathsf{X}^{\xi}
. We compare
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}
to
V_{N}
.

Lemma 6.89

Suppose
V_{N}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\infty
, and define
\xi_{N}
as in Lemma
6.87
.

(1)

Suppose
R>0
and
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N}^{R},{\widehat{b}}_{N}^{R}]
for all
N
,
then
\widetilde{V}^{\xi_{N}}_{N}\asymp V_{N}
as
N\to\infty
.

(2)

If
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then
\widetilde{V}^{\xi_{N}}_{N}\sim V_{N}
as
N\to\infty
.

(3)

\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}\sim V_{N}
as
N\to\infty
uniformly on compact subsets of
\xi
: For every
\epsilon>0
there are
\xi^{\ast}>0
and
N_{0}>1
, so that
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}/V_{N}\in[e^{-\epsilon},e^{\epsilon}]
for all
|\xi|<\xi^{\ast},N>N_{0}
.

Proof

Part (1) is because of Lemma
6.86
(3) and the bound
|\xi_{N}|\leq R+1
from Lemma
6.86
. Part (2) follows from part (3) and Corollary
6.88
. It remains to prove part (3).

To do this we decompose
S_{N}
into weakly correlated large blocks of roughly the same
\mathsf{X}
-variance, and check that the
\mathsf{X}^{\xi}
-variance of the
i
-th block converges
uniformly
in
i
to its
\mathsf{X}
-variance.

Let
\{X_{n}\}
and
\{\widetilde{X}_{n}^{\xi}\}
denote the Markov chains with transition kernels
\{\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy)\}
,
\{\widetilde{\pi}^{\xi}_{n,n+1}(x,dy)\}
and initial distribution
\mu_{1}(dx)
. Given natural numbers
n>m
, let

\displaystyle S_{n,m}

\displaystyle:=X_{n}+\cdots+X_{m-1}

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{n,m}^{\xi}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{n}+\cdots+\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{m-1}

\displaystyle p_{n,m}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=p_{n}(\xi)+\cdots+p_{m-1}(\xi).

Notice that for all
R>0
,
n<m
, and
|\xi|\leq R
,

p_{n,m}(0)=0,\hskip 11.74988ptp_{n,m}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{n,m}),\hskip 11.74988pt|p_{n,m}^{\prime}(\xi)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n,m})|\leq C(R).

(20.24)

The first identity is because
\overline{h}_{n}(\cdot,0)\equiv 1
,
\overline{p}_{n}(0)=1
by the uniqueness of the fundamental solution.
The second identity is because

p_{n,m}^{\prime}(0)=P_{m-1}^{\prime}(0)-P_{n-1}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{m-1})-\mathbb{E}(S_{n-1})

by choice of
\{a_{n}\}
. The inequality can be proved by applying Lemma
6.86
to the shifted Markov chain
\{X_{k}\}_{k\geq n}
.

Let
V(S_{n,m}):={\rm Var}(S_{n,m})
.
The application of Lemma
6.86
to the shifted Markov chain
\{X_{k}\}_{k\geq n}
also gives a constant
M_{0}
s.t. for all
|\xi|\leq R
,

V(S_{n,m})\geq M_{0}\Rightarrow\begin{cases}C(R)^{-1}\leq\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n,m})/V(S_{n,m})\leq C(R)&\\
2^{-1}\leq p_{n,m}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)/\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n,m})\leq 2.\end{cases}

(20.25)

M_{0}
is independent of
n
: It is a function of
R
,
K
,
\epsilon_{0}
, and the uniform bounds on
h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
and its derivatives.

Step 1 (uniform exponential mixing).

There are

C_{mix}^{\ast}=C_{mix}(R)>0
,
\eta=\eta(R)\in(0,1)
such that for every
|\xi|\leq R
, for all
n<m
,

\bigl|\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(f_{m}(\widetilde{X}_{m}^{\xi},\widetilde{X}_{m+1}^{\xi}),f_{n}(\widetilde{X}_{n}^{\xi},\widetilde{X}_{n+1}^{\xi})\bigr)\bigr|\leq C_{mix}^{\ast}\eta^{m-n}.

Proof: If
|\xi|\leq R
, then the Markov chain
\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{n}
is uniformly elliptic with ellipticity constant
\epsilon_{0}(R)>0
.
The step follows from Proposition
1.11
.

Step 2 (Block decomposition).

For every
\epsilon>0
small enough, for every
R>1
, there exists
M>1
and integers
n_{i}\uparrow\infty
such that:

(1)

M\leq V(S_{n_{i},n_{i+1}})\leq 2M
;

(2)

|{\rm Cov}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n_{i+1}},\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{j},n_{j+1}})|\leq C_{mix}^{\#}\eta^{n_{j}-n_{i+1}}
for all
|\xi|\leq R
and
i<j
, where the constant
C_{mix}^{\#}
is independent of
M,i,j
;

(3)

For all
|\xi|\leq R
, for all
i>3
, for all
n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
,

e^{-\epsilon}\leq\frac{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{1,n})}{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}})+\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}}\leq e^{\epsilon}.

(20.26)

(4)

\displaystyle M^{\ast}:=\sup_{i}\sup_{n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]}\sup_{|\xi|\leq R}|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|<\infty

Proof.
We write
V_{n,m}:=V(S_{n,m})
and

\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n,m}:=\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}_{n,m}^{\xi})
, and fix

M>\max\left\{2\left(K^{2}+\frac{C^{\ast}_{mix}}{1-\eta}\right),\frac{4C_{mix}^{\ast}C(R)}{\epsilon^{-1}(1-\eta)^{3}}\right\}.

Construct
n_{i}=n_{i}(M)\in\mathbb{N}
by induction as follows:
n_{1}:=1
, and

n_{i+1}:=\min\{n>n_{i}:V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}>M\}.

There does indeed exist
n>n_{i}
with
V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}>M
, because
V_{n_{i},n}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\infty
, as can be seen from the following calculation:

\displaystyle\infty\xleftarrow[\infty\leftarrow\ n]{}V_{1,n}

\displaystyle=V_{1,n_{i}}+V_{n_{i},n}+2{\rm Cov}(S_{1,n_{i}},S_{n_{i},n})

\displaystyle=V_{n_{i},n}+V_{1,n_{i}}+O\left(\sum_{m=1}^{n_{i}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|{\rm Cov}(X_{m},X_{n_{i}+k})|\right)

\displaystyle=V_{n_{i},n}+O(1),\text{ by step 1 with $\xi=0$.}

By construction,
V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}>M
, and

\displaystyle V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}\leq V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}-1}+|V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}-V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}-1}|

\displaystyle\leq M+|V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}-V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}-1}|\ \ \text{ by the minimality of $n_{i+1}$}

\displaystyle\leq M+V(f_{n_{i+1}-2}(X_{n_{i+1}-2},X_{n_{i+1}-1}))

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +2|{\rm Cov}(f_{n_{i+1}-2}(X_{n_{i+1}-2},X_{n_{i+1}-1}),S_{n_{i+1}-1})|

\displaystyle\leq M+2\left(K^{2}+\frac{C^{\ast}_{mix}}{1-\eta}\right)\leq 2M\ \ \text{ by the choice of $M$}.

So
M<V_{n_{i},n_{i+1}}\leq 2M
, and
\{n_{i}\}
satisfies part (1).

If
i<j
, then
\displaystyle|{\rm Cov}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n_{i+1}},\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{j},n_{j+1}})|\leq\sum_{k=n_{i}}^{n_{i+1}-1}\sum_{\ell=n_{j}}^{n_{j+1}-1}C_{mix}^{\ast}\eta^{\ell-k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{mix}^{\ast}\sum_{k=n_{i}}^{n_{i+1}-1}\frac{\eta^{n_{j}-k}}{1-\eta}=\frac{C^{\ast}_{mix}\eta^{n_{j}-n_{i+1}}}{1-\eta}\sum_{k=n_{i}}^{n_{i+1}-1}\eta^{n_{i+1}-k}=\frac{C_{mix}^{\ast}\eta^{n_{j}-n_{i+1}}}{(1-\eta)^{2}}.

Part (2) follows with
C_{mix}^{\#}:={C_{mix}^{\ast}}/{(1-\eta)^{2}}
.

Part (3) follows from parts (1),(2). Namely, fix
n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
, then

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{1,n})-\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}}-\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\!\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq i-1}|{\rm Cov}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}},\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{\ell},n_{\ell+1}})|+2\sum_{1\leq k\leq i-1}|{\rm Cov}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}},\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq i-1}C_{mix}^{\#}\eta^{n_{\ell}-n_{k+1}}+2\sum_{1\leq k\leq i-1}C_{mix}^{\#}\eta^{n_{i}-n_{k+1}}\leq 2\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq i}C_{mix}^{\#}\eta^{\ell-k-1}

\displaystyle=2C_{mix}^{\#}\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{i-1}\eta^{\ell-k-1}\leq\frac{2C_{mix}^{\#}i}{1-\eta}=\frac{2C_{mix}^{\ast}i}{(1-\eta)^{3}}.

By (
20.25
),

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{k}n_{k+1}}\geq\frac{M(i-1)}{C(R)}.

So

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i}-1})}{\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{k}n_{k+1}}+\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}}-1\right|\leq\frac{\left(\frac{2C_{mix}^{\ast}}{(1-\eta)^{3}}\right)i}{C(R)^{-1}M(i-1)}\leq\frac{1}{M}\cdot\frac{2C_{mix}^{\ast}C(R)}{\left(1-\eta\right)^{3}}\cdot\frac{i}{i-1}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\cdot\frac{i}{i-1},

where the last inequality is by the choice of
M
. If
i>3
, the last bound is less than
\frac{3}{4}\epsilon
, and (
20.26
) follows for all
\epsilon
sufficiently small.

Part (4) is a uniform bound on
|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
n\in[n_{i},n]
,
|\xi|\leq R
.
By construction,
V_{n_{i},n}\leq 2M
. By Theorem
2.30
, this implies a uniform upper bound on
\sum_{k=n_{i}}^{n-1}u_{k}^{2}
. The structure constants of
\{X_{n}\}
and
\{\widetilde{X}_{n}^{\xi}\}
are equal up to a bounded multiplicative error. So the same theorem, applied to the Markov chain
\{\widetilde{X}^{\xi}_{k}\}_{k\geq n_{i}}
, gives a uniform upper bound for
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}
, whence

\sup_{i}\sup_{n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]}\sup_{|\xi|\leq R}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}<\infty.

A routine modification of the argument we used to show (
20.17
) shows that

\left|p_{n,m}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}\left[\left(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n})+O(1)\right)^{2}\right]\right|\leq\text{const}.

The expectation term is uniformly bounded because of the bound on
\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}
and the Minkowski inequality, so part (4) follows.

Step 3 (Block expectation).

For every
\epsilon>0
there exists
\xi^{\ast}>0
such that for all
|\xi|\leq\xi^{\ast}
,

\left|\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n})-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n})\right|\leq\epsilon\text{ for all $i\in\mathbb{N}$ and $n_{i}\leq n\leq n_{i+1}$.}

Proof.

By Lemma
6.82

h_{k}(\cdot,\xi)
is uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity when
|\xi|\leq R
. By Lemma
6.84
,
\xi\mapsto h_{k}(\cdot,\xi)
is uniformly Lipschitz on
[-R,R]
. It follows that

\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(x,\xi)}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}1\text{ uniformly for $i\in\mathbb{N}$, $n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]$, $(x,y)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n_{i}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{n+1}$.}

In particular, there is a
\xi^{\ast}_{1}
s.t. for all
|\xi|\leq\xi^{\ast}_{1}

2^{-1}\leq\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(x,\xi)}\leq 2\text{ for all $i\in\mathbb{N}$, $n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]$, and $(x,y)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n_{i}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{n}$.}

This has a useful consequence. Since

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left(e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\right)\right)=\mathbb{E}(1)=1,

2^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\right)\leq e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\right)\text{ whenever }|\xi|\leq\xi^{\ast}_{1}.

(20.27)

Fix
L>0
and let
A_{L}:=[|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n})|\leq L]
, then:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi}_{X_{n_{i}}}(\widetilde{S}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n})-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})={\mathbb{E}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n}))e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n}))e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\cdot 1_{A_{L}}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n}))e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{L}}\right).

Expectation of the first summand
:
M^{\ast}:=\sup\limits_{i}\sup\limits_{n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]}\sup\limits_{|\xi|\leq R}|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|<\infty
. Therefore by (
20.24
), for all
|\xi|\leq R
,
n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
,

\displaystyle p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)

\displaystyle=p_{n_{i},n}(0)+\xi p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime}(0)+O(\xi^{2})=\xi{\mathbb{E}}(S_{n_{i},n})+O(\xi^{2}),

(20.28)

where
|O(\xi^{2})|\leq M^{\ast}\xi^{2}
.

So on
A_{L}
,
|\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)|\leq|\xi|\cdot|S_{n_{i},n}-{\mathbb{E}}(S_{n_{i},n})|+M^{\ast}\xi^{2}\leq L|\xi|+M^{\ast}\xi^{2}
, uniformly in
i,n\in[n_{i},n]
. In particular,

e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}1\text{ on $A_{L}$, uniformly in $i$, $n\in[n_{i},n]$. }

Together with the uniform convergence
\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}1
, this implies that the first summand converges to

\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}[(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))1_{A_{L}}]
uniformly in
i
,
X_{n_{i}}
, and
n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
.

The expectation of the limit satisfies

|\mathbb{E}[(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))1_{A_{L}}]|=|\mathbb{E}[(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))1_{A_{L}^{c}}]|

\leq\mathbb{E}[L^{-1}(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}^{c}}]\leq\frac{V(S_{n_{i},n})}{L}\leq\frac{2M}{L}.

Thus, for every
\epsilon>0
, for every
L
large enough, for all
|\xi|
sufficiently small, for all
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
, the first summand has expectation
<\epsilon/2
.

Expectation of the second summand
: Fix
0<\delta\ll\xi_{1}^{\ast}
. Assume
L
is so large s.t.

|t|<\delta e^{\delta|t|}
for all
|t|>L
.

Decompose
A^{c}_{L}:=A^{c}_{+}\uplus A^{c}_{-}
, where
A^{c}_{+}:=[S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})>L]
and
A^{c}_{+}:=[S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})<-L]
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left(|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})|e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{+}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left(|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})|e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{+}}\right),\text{ provided $|\xi|\leq\xi^{\ast}_{1}$}

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n}))e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{+}}\right)\big/\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}),\text{ by \eqref{exponential-moment}}

\displaystyle=4\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n}))e^{\xi(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{+}}\right)\bigg/\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))})

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta\mathbb{E}_{X_{n_{i}}}(e^{(\xi+\delta)(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))})\big/\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi(S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))})

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta\exp\left({p_{n_{i},n}(\xi+\delta)-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)-\delta\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n})}\right),\text{ provided $|\xi+\delta|<\xi^{\ast}_{1}$}.

(see (
20.27
)).
Expanding
p_{n_{i},n}(\xi+\delta)
into Taylor series around
\xi
, and recalling
|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)|\leq M^{\ast}
for
|\xi|\leq R
, we find that the term in the exponent is bounded above by

\displaystyle\delta|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime}(\xi)-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n})|+M^{\ast}\delta^{2}=\delta|p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime}(\xi)-p_{n_{i},n}^{\prime}(0)|+M^{\ast}\delta^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M^{\ast}(\delta|\xi|+\delta^{2})\leq M^{\ast}(R\delta+\delta^{2}),

which can be made as small as we wish by choosing
\delta
properly.

The conclusion is that for all
L
large enough, for all
|\xi|
sufficiently small, for all
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
,

\mathbb{E}\left(|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})|e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{+}}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

Similarly, one can show that for all
L
large enough, for all
|\xi|
sufficiently small, for all
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
,

\mathbb{E}\left(|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}({S}_{n_{i},n})|e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}-p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}\cdot 1_{A^{c}_{-}}\right)<\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

Thus, for every
\epsilon>0
, for all
L
sufficiently large, for all
|\xi|
sufficiently small, for all
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
, the expectation of the second summand is less than
\epsilon/2
in absolute value.

Step 4 (Block variance).

For every
\epsilon>0
there exists
\xi^{\ast}>0
such that for all
|\xi|\leq\xi^{\ast}
,

\left|\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}-V_{n_{i},n}\right|\leq\epsilon\text{ for all $i\in\mathbb{N}$ and $n_{i}\leq n\leq n_{i+1}$.}

Proof.
The proof is similar to the proof of step 3. Fix
L
to be determined later and let

A_{L}:=[|S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n})|\leq L]
, then

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}^{\xi}(\widetilde{S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi})={\mathbb{E}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(\widetilde{S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi}))^{2}e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}1_{A_{L}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+{\mathbb{E}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(\widetilde{S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi}))^{2}e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}1_{A_{L}^{c}}\right).

The second summand can be analyzed as in step 3, this time with the inequality
t^{2}<\delta e^{\delta|t|}
for all
|t|
large enough. The conclusion is that for every
\epsilon>0
, for all
L
sufficiently large, for all
|\xi|
sufficiently small, for all
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
,

{\mathbb{E}}\left((S_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(\widetilde{S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi}))^{2}e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}1_{A^{c}_{L}}\right)<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(20.29)

The first summand converges to
\mathbb{E}((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}})
as
\xi\to 0
uniformly in
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
because

\bullet

e^{\xi S_{n_{i},n}}\cdot\frac{h_{n+1}(X_{n+1},\xi)}{e^{p_{n_{i},n}(\xi)}h_{n_{i}}(X_{n_{i}},\xi)}1_{A_{L}}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}1_{A_{L}}

uniformly in
i\in\mathbb{N},n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
, see the proof of step 3; and

\bullet

(S_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi}))^{2}1_{A_{L}}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}(S_{n_{i},n}-{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}}

uniformly in
i,n\in[n_{i},n]
, because for some
t
between
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}(\widetilde{S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi})
and
{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n})
,

\displaystyle\bigl|(S_{n_{i},n}-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi}))^{2}-(S_{n_{i},n}-{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}))^{2}\bigr|

\displaystyle=2|S_{n_{i},n}-t||\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi})-{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n})|

\displaystyle\leq 2(L+|\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi})-{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n})|)|\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n}^{\xi})-{\mathbb{E}}({S}_{n_{i},n})|\text{ on $A_{L}$}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}0\text{ uniformly on $A_{L}$ in $i\in\mathbb{N},n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]$, by step 3.}

The limit of the first summand
\mathbb{E}((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}})\xrightarrow[L\to\infty]{}V_{n_{i},n}
uniformly in
i,n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
.
Indeed, applying (
20.29
) with
\xi=0

|V_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}})|=\mathbb{E}((S_{n_{i},n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{n_{i},n}))^{2}1_{A_{L}^{c}})<\frac{\epsilon}{2}

for all
L
large enough, for all
i\in\mathbb{N},n\in[n_{i},n_{i+1}]
.
Step 4 follows.

Proof of part (3) of the Lemma.
Fix
\epsilon>0
, and construct the block decomposition as in step 2.

By step 4 there exists
\xi^{\ast}>0
s.t. for all
|\xi|<\xi^{\ast}
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N},n\in[n_{k},n_{k+1}]
,

e^{-\epsilon}V_{n_{k},n}\leq\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n}\leq e^{\epsilon}V_{n_{k},n}
. Therefore

e^{-\epsilon}\leq\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}}+\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n_{i},n}}{\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}{V}_{n_{k},n_{k+1}}+{V}_{n_{i},n}}\leq e^{\epsilon}.

By part (3) of step 2, for all
n>n_{3}
, for all
|\xi|<\xi^{\ast}
,

e^{-3\epsilon}\leq{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{n}}\big/{V_{n}}\leq e^{3\epsilon}.

\square

20.5
Asymptotics of the log moment generating functions

We need an elementary observation from probability theory.
Let
X,Y
be two random variables on the same probability space
(\Omega,\mathfs F,{\mathbb{P}})
. Suppose
X
has finite non-zero variance, and
Y
is positive and bounded. Let

{\rm Var}^{Y}(X)
be the variance of
X
with respect to the change of measure
\frac{Y}{\mathbb{E}(Y)}d{\mathbb{P}}
, i.e.

{\rm Var}^{Y}(X):=\frac{\mathbb{E}(X^{2}Y)}{\mathbb{E}(Y)}-\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(XY)}{\mathbb{E}(Y)}\right)^{2}.

Lemma 6.90

Suppose
0<{\rm Var}(X)<\infty
and
C^{-1}\leq Y\leq C
with
C
a positive constant, then

\displaystyle C^{-4}\leq\frac{{\rm Var}^{Y}(X)}{{\rm Var}(X)}\leq C^{4}.

Proof

For every random variable
W
, if
W_{1},W_{2}
are two independent copies of
W
then
{\rm Var}(W)=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(W_{1}-W_{2})^{2}]
. In particular, if
(X_{1},Y_{1})
,
(X_{2},Y_{2})
are two independent copies of the random vector
(X,Y)
, then

\displaystyle{\rm Var}^{Y}(X)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(X_{1}-X_{2})^{2}Y_{1}Y_{2}]}{\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}Y_{2})}=C^{\pm 4}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(X_{1}-X_{2})^{2}]=C^{\pm 4}{\rm Var}(X).

\square

Proof of Theorem
19.1
on the asymptotic behavior of
\mathfs F_{N}(\xi):=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})
:
Let
\mathsf{f}
be an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional on a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
X
, s.t.
V_{N}:={\rm Var}(S_{N})\neq 0
for
N\geq N_{0}
.

Since
\|S_{N}\|_{\infty}<\infty
, we may differentiate under the expectation and obtain that for all
k
,

\frac{d^{k}}{d\xi^{k}}\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})=\mathbb{E}(S_{N}^{k}e^{\xi S_{N}})
. A direct calculation now shows that

\displaystyle\mathfs\displaystyle F_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N}e^{\xi S_{N}})}{\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})}=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\mathbb{E}^{Y_{N}^{\xi}}(S_{N}),

\displaystyle\mathfs\displaystyle F_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N}^{2}e^{\xi S_{N}})}{\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})}-\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N}e^{\xi S_{N}})}{\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{{\rm Var}^{Y_{N}^{\xi}}(S_{N})}{{\rm Var}(S_{N})},\text{ where $Y_{N}^{\xi}:=e^{\xi S_{N}}.$}

Part 1:
Substituting
\xi=0
gives
\mathfs F_{N}(0)=0
,

\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
,
\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}(0)=1
.

Part 2:

\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)=0

\Leftrightarrow

{\rm Var}^{Y_{N}^{\xi}}(S_{N})=0

\Leftrightarrow

S_{N}=const

\frac{Y_{N}^{\xi}}{\mathbb{E}(Y_{N}^{\xi})}d{\mathbb{P}}
–a.s.
\Leftrightarrow

S_{N}=const

{\mathbb{P}}
–a.s.
\Leftrightarrow

{\rm Var}(S_{N})=0
. So
\mathcal{F}_{N}
is strictly convex on
\mathbb{R}
for all
N>N_{0}
.

Part 3:

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}(S_{N})}{{\rm Var}(S_{N})}\equiv\frac{{\rm Var}^{Z_{N}}(S_{N})}{{\rm Var}(S_{N})},

where
Z_{N}^{\xi}:=e^{\xi S_{N}}\frac{h_{N+1}^{\xi}}{h_{1}^{\xi}}
(the normalization constant does not matter). Next,

Z_{N}^{\xi}\equiv Y_{N}^{\xi}W_{N}^{\xi}
, where
W_{N}^{\xi}:=h^{\xi}_{N+1}/h^{\xi}_{1}
.
Lemma
6.82
says that for every
R>0
there is a constant
C=C(R)
s.t.
C^{-1}\leq W_{N}^{\xi}\leq C
for all
N
and
|\xi|\leq R
. Lemma
6.84
and the obvious identity
h_{n}^{0}\equiv 1
imply that
W_{N}^{\xi}\xrightarrow[\xi\to 0]{}1
uniformly in
N
. So there is no loss of generality in assuming that
C(R)\xrightarrow[R\to 0]{}1
.

By Lemma
6.90
with the probability measure
\frac{e^{\xi S_{N}}}{\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})}d{\mathbb{P}}
and
Y=W_{N}^{\xi}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}(S_{N})}{V_{N}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)}=\frac{{\rm Var}^{Y_{N}^{\xi}W_{N}^{\xi}}(S_{N})}{{\rm Var}^{Y_{N}^{\xi}}(S_{N})}\in\left[{C(R)^{-4}},C(R)^{4}\right],\text{ }\forall|\xi|\leq R,\;\;N\geq 1.

(20.30)

By Lemma
6.86
(3),

\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}(S_{N})\asymp V_{N}
uniformly on compact sets of
\xi
, and by Lemma
6.89

for every
\epsilon
there exists
\delta,N_{\epsilon}>0
s.t.
e^{-\epsilon}<\widetilde{V}^{\xi}_{N}(S_{N})/V_{N}<e^{\epsilon}
for all
N>N_{\epsilon}
and
|\xi|\leq\delta
.
It follows that for every
R
there exists
C_{2}(R)>1
such that
C_{2}(R)\xrightarrow[R\to 0]{}1
and

C_{2}(R)^{-1}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\leq C_{2}(R)\text{ for all }|\xi|\leq R.

Part 4:
Suppose
\epsilon>0
. We saw in part 3 that there exist
\delta,N_{\epsilon}
s.t.

e^{-\epsilon}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\leq e^{\epsilon}
for all
|\xi|\leq\delta,N\geq N_{\epsilon}.

Recall that
\mathcal{F}_{N}(0)=0
and
\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbb{E}(S_{N})/V_{N}
. So for all
|\xi|\leq\delta
,

\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}_{N}(0)+\int_{0}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)+\int_{\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)}^{\eta}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)d\alpha\right)d\eta.

Since
\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}=e^{\pm\epsilon}
on
[-\delta,\delta]
and
|\eta|\leq|\xi|\leq\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\xi+\frac{1}{2}e^{\pm\epsilon}\left(\xi-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}
.

\Box

20.6
Asymptotics of the rate functions.

The
rate functions

\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)
are the Legendre transforms of
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\log\mathbb{E}(e^{\xi S_{N}})
.
Recall that the Legendre transform
of a strictly convex function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is the function
\varphi^{\ast}:(\inf\varphi^{\prime},\sup\varphi^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}
,

\varphi^{\ast}(\eta)=\xi\eta-\varphi(\xi)\text{ for the unique $\xi$ s.t. $\varphi^{\prime}(\xi)=\eta$}.

On its domain,
\varphi^{\ast}(\eta)=\max\{\xi\eta-\varphi(\xi)\}
.

Lemma 6.91

Suppose
\varphi(\xi)
is strictly convex and twice differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
, and let
\varphi^{\prime}(\pm\infty):=\lim\limits_{\xi\to\pm\infty}\varphi^{\prime}(\xi)
. Then the Legendre transform
\varphi^{\ast}
is strictly convex and twice differentiable on
(\varphi^{\prime}(-\infty),\varphi^{\prime}(+\infty))
, and for every
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,

\varphi^{\ast}(\varphi^{\prime}(t))=t\varphi^{\prime}(t)-\varphi(t),\ (\varphi^{\ast})^{\prime}(\varphi^{\prime}(t))=t\text{ , }(\varphi^{\ast})^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{\prime}(t))=\frac{1}{\varphi^{\prime\prime}(t)}

(20.31)

Proof

Under the assumptions of the lemma,
\varphi^{\prime}
is strictly increasing and differentiable. So
(\varphi^{\prime})^{-1}:(\varphi^{\prime}(-\infty),\varphi^{\prime}(\infty))\to\mathbb{R}
is well-defined, strictly increasing and differentiable, and

\varphi^{\ast}(\eta)=\eta(\varphi^{\prime})^{-1}(\eta)-\varphi[(\varphi^{\prime})^{-1}(\eta)]

The lemma follows by differentiation of right-hand-side.

\square

Proof of Theorem
19.2
on the asymptotics of the rate functions
\mathfs I_{N}:=\mathfs F_{N}^{\ast}
:

Part 1:
Since
\mathfs F_{N}
is strictly convex and smooth,
\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}
is strictly increasing and continuous. So

\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}[-1,1]=[\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-1),\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(1)]\equiv[a_{N}^{1},b_{N}^{1}]
, and
for every
\eta\in[a_{N}^{1},b_{N}^{1}]
, there exists a unique
\xi\in[-1,1]
such that
\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi)=\eta
. So
\mathrm{dom}(\mathfs I_{N})\supset[a_{N}^{1},b_{N}^{1}]
.

By Theorem
19.1
there is

C>0
such that
C^{-1}\leq\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}\leq C
on
[-1,1]
for all
N\geq N_{0}
.
Since
\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
and

\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\rho)=\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)+\int_{0}^{\rho}\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi)d\xi
, we have

b_{N}^{1}\equiv\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(1)\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+C^{-1}\ ,\ a_{N}^{1}\equiv\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(-1)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-C^{-1}.

So

\mathrm{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{N})\supseteq[a_{N}^{1},b_{N}^{1}]\supseteq\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-C^{-1},\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+C^{-1}\right]\text{ for all }N\geq N_{0}.

Part 2
follows from Lemma
6.91

and the strict convexity of
\mathcal{F}_{N}
on
[-R,R].

Part 3:
Let
J_{N}:=\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-C^{-1},\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+C^{-1}\right]
. In part 1 we constructed functions

\xi_{N}:J_{N}\to[-1,1]
such that

\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N}(\eta))=\eta.

Clearly
\xi_{N}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)=0
.
Recalling that
C^{-1}\leq\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}\leq C
on
[-1,1]
, we see that
\xi_{N}^{\prime}(\eta)=\frac{1}{\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{N}(\eta))}\in[C^{-1},C]
on
J_{N}
. Hence

|\xi_{N}(\eta)|\leq C|\eta-\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}|\text{ for all $\eta\in J_{N}$, $N\geq N_{0}$}.

Fix
0<\epsilon<1
. By
Theorem
19.1
(4) there are
\delta,N_{\epsilon}>0
s.t.
e^{-\epsilon}\leq\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}\leq e^{\epsilon}
on
[-\delta,\delta]
for all
N>N_{\epsilon}
. If

|\eta-\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}|<\delta/C
, then

|\xi_{N}(\eta)|<\delta
, and
\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{N}(\eta))\in[e^{-\epsilon},e^{\epsilon}]
.

Since
\mathcal{F}_{N}(0)=0
and
\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}
, we have by
(
20.31
) that
\mathfs I_{N}(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})=\mathfs I_{N}^{\prime}(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})=0
and

\mathcal{I}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)=1/\mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{N}(\eta))\in[e^{-\epsilon},e^{\epsilon}].

Writing

\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)=\mathcal{I}_{N}(\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})+\int_{\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}}^{\eta}\left(\mathcal{I}_{N}^{\prime}(\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})+\int_{\tfrac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}}^{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\beta)d\beta\right)d\alpha,

we find that
\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)=e^{\pm\epsilon}\frac{1}{2}(\eta-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}})^{2}
for all

\eta
s.t.
|\eta-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}|\leq\delta/C
.

Part 4:
If
\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\to 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\in\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-\delta_{N},\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+\delta_{N}\right]

with
\delta_{N}\to 0
. By part 3,
\mathcal{I}_{N}(\tfrac{z_{N}}{V_{N}})\sim\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)^{2}
, whence

V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}(\tfrac{z_{N}}{V_{N}})\sim\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{z_{n}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\right)^{2}
.

\Box

Let
H_{N}(\eta)
denote the Legendre transform of
P_{N}(\xi)/V_{N}
.
We will compare
H_{N}(\eta)
to
\mathcal{I}_{N}(\eta)
. This is needed to link the change of measure we performed in section §
20.3
to the functions
\mathcal{I}_{N}
which appear in the statement of the local limit theorem for large deviations.

Lemma 6.92

Suppose
R>0
and
V_{N}\neq 0
for all
N
large enough. Then

(1)

H_{N}
is well-defined and real-analytic on

\left[\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(-R)}{V_{N}},\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(R)}{V_{N}}\right]
for all
N
large enough.

(2)

There exists
c>0
such that

H_{N}(\cdot)
is well-defined and real-analytic on

\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-c,\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+c\right)
for all
N
large enough.

Proof

Lemma
6.87

and its proof provide
real analytic maps

\xi_{N}:\biggl[\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(-R)}{V_{N}},\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(R)}{V_{N}}\biggr]\to[-R,R]\hskip 11.74988pt\text{s.t.}\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N}(\eta))}{V_{N}}=\eta.

Hence

\displaystyle H_{N}(\eta)=\frac{1}{V_{N}}\left[\xi_{N}(\eta)P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi(\eta))-P_{N}(\xi(\eta))\right]

is well-defined and real-analytic on the interval
[\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(-R)}{V_{N}},\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(R)}{V_{N}}]
.
This proves part (1). Part (2) follows from Lemma
6.87
(2).

\square

Lemma 6.93

Suppose
V_{N}\neq 0
for all
N\geq N_{0}
,
then
\exists c>0
such that

(1)

\mathrm{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{N})\cap\mathrm{dom}(H_{N})\supset\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-c,\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+c\right]
for all
N\geq N_{0}
.

(2)

Recall that
[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]=[\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-R),\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(R)]
. For every
R>0
there exists
C(R)>0
s.t. if
z/V_{N}\in[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]
and
N\geq N_{0}
, then

\bigl|V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}(\tfrac{z}{V_{N}})-V_{N}H_{N}(\tfrac{z}{V_{N}})\bigr|\leq C(R).

(3)

For every
\epsilon>0
,
\exists\delta,N_{\epsilon}>0
s.t. if
N\geq N_{\epsilon}
and
\left|\frac{z-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right|<\delta
, then

\bigl|V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}(\tfrac{z}{V_{N}})-V_{N}H_{N}(\tfrac{z}{V_{N}})\bigr|\leq\epsilon.

Proof

Part (1) is a direct consequence of Lemma
6.92
and Theorem
19.2
(1).

To prove the other parts of the lemma, we use the following consequence of
Lemma
6.86
(6): For every
R>0
, for all
N
large enough, for every

\eta\in[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]
,
there exist

\xi_{N}^{(1)},\xi_{N}^{(2)}\in[-(R+1),(R+1)]
such that

\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N}^{(1)})}{V_{N}}=\eta\ ,\ \mathcal{F}_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N}^{(2)})=\eta.

Arguing as in the proof of part 3 of Theorem
19.2
, we can also find a constant
C(R)
such that
|\xi_{N}^{(i)}|\leq C(R)\bigl|\eta-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\bigr|
.

It is a general fact that the Legendre transform of a convex function
\varphi
is equal on its domain to
\varphi^{\ast}(\eta)=\sup\limits_{\xi}\{\xi\eta-\varphi(\xi)\}
. Thus for every

z\in[a_{N}^{R}V_{N},b_{N}^{R}V_{N}]
,

\displaystyle V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)=V_{N}\sup_{\xi}\left\{\xi\frac{z}{V_{N}}-\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi)\right\}=V_{N}\left(\xi_{N}^{(2)}\frac{z}{V_{N}}-\mathcal{F}_{N}(\xi_{N}^{(2)})\right)

\displaystyle\leq V_{N}\biggl(\xi_{N}^{(2)}\frac{z}{V_{N}}-\frac{P_{N}(\xi_{N}^{(2)})}{V_{N}}\biggr)+\Delta_{N}\left(R+1\right),\text{ see Lemma \ref{Lemma-Changed-Expectation-Variance}(5)}

\displaystyle\leq V_{N}\sup_{\xi}\left\{\xi\frac{z}{V_{N}}-\frac{P_{N}(\xi)}{V_{N}}\right\}+\Delta_{N}(R+1)\equiv V_{N}H_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)+\Delta_{N}(R+1).

So
V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\bigl(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\bigr)-V_{N}H_{N}\bigl(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\bigr)\leq\Delta_{N}(R+1)
.

Similarly, one can show that

V_{N}H_{N}\bigl(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\bigr)-V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\bigl(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\bigr)\leq\Delta_{N}(R+1)
, whence

\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\sup_{z\in\left[a_{N}^{R}V_{N},b_{N}^{R}V_{N}\right]}\left|V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)-V_{N}H_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)\right|\leq\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\Delta_{N}(R+1).

Part (2) now follows from Lemma
6.86
(5).

If instead of taking
z/V_{N}\in[a_{N}^{R},b_{N}^{R}]
we take

\displaystyle z/V_{N}\in\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}-\delta,\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}+\delta\right)
,
then
|\xi_{N}^{(i)}|<C\delta
, and the same argument will show that

\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\sup_{\left|\frac{z-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right|\leq\delta}\left|V_{N}\mathcal{I}_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)-V_{N}H_{N}\left(\frac{z}{V_{N}}\right)\right|\leq\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\Delta_{N}(C\delta).

Part (3) follows from Lemma
6.86
(5).

\square

20.7
The local limit theorem for large deviations.

Proof of Theorem
19.5
.
We give the proof in the non-lattice case; the modifications needed for the lattice case are routine.

Suppose
\mathsf{f}
is an a.s. uniformly bounded additive functional of a uniformly elliptic Markov chain
\mathsf{X}
. We assume that
\mathsf{f}
is irreducible, and that
\mathsf{f}
has algebraic range
\mathbb{R}
.
In this case
\mathsf{f}
is not center-tight, and
V_{N}:={\rm Var}(S_{N})\to\infty
(see §
5
). There is no loss of generality in assuming that
V_{N}\neq 0
for all
N
.

Recall that
[{\widehat{a}}_{N},{\widehat{b}}_{N}]=[\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(-R)-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}},\mathfs F_{N}^{\prime}(R)-\frac{\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}]
, and suppose

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\in[{\widehat{a}}_{N},{\widehat{b}}_{N}].

Let
h_{n}^{\xi}(\cdot):=h_{n}(\cdot,\xi)
,
p_{n}(\xi)
, and
P_{N}(\xi)
be as in §§
20.2
,
20.3
. The assumption on
z_{N}
allows us to construct

\xi_{N}\in\left[-(R+1),(R+1)\right]
as in Lemma
6.87
:

P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})=z_{N}\text{ and }\xi_{N}=O\left(\tfrac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(S_{N})}{V_{N}}\right).

Define a Markov array

\widetilde{\mathsf{X}}:=\{\widetilde{X}^{(N)}_{n}:1\leq n\leq N+1\}
with
state spaces
(\mathfrak{S}_{n},\mathfs B(\mathfrak{S}_{n}),\mu_{n})
(the state spaces of
\mathsf{X}
), and transition probabilities

\widetilde{\pi}^{(N)}_{n,n+1}(x,dy):=e^{\xi_{N}f_{n}(x,y)}\frac{h_{n+1}(y,\xi_{N})}{e^{p_{n}(\xi_{N})}h_{n}(x,\xi_{N})}\cdot\pi_{n,n+1}(x,dy).

Let
\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}=\{f^{(N)}_{n}:1\leq n\leq N+1,N\in\mathbb{N}\}
where
f^{(N)}_{n}:=f_{n}
, and set

\widetilde{S}_{N}:=f_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1}^{(N)},\widetilde{X}_{2}^{(N)})+\cdots+f_{N}(\widetilde{X}^{(N)}_{N},\widetilde{X}_{N+1}^{(N)}).

Recall that
e^{\xi_{N}f_{n}}
,
h_{n}
, and
e^{p_{n}(\xi_{N})}
are uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity, by the assumption on
\mathsf{f}
, and Lemma
6.82
. So
\widetilde{\pi}^{(N)}_{n,n+1}(x,dy)
differ from
{\pi}_{n,n+1}(x,dy)
by densities which are bounded away from zero and infinity uniformly in
N
.
It follows that
\widetilde{\mathsf{X}}
is uniformly elliptic,
\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}
is a.s. uniformly bounded, and the structure constants of
(\widetilde{\mathsf{X}},\widetilde{\mathsf{f}})
are equal to the structure constants of
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
up to a uniformly bounded multiplicative error. Thus

(1)

(\widetilde{\mathsf{X}},\widetilde{\mathsf{f}})
and
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{f})
have the same algebraic ranges, co-ranges, and essential ranges. In particular,
(\widetilde{\mathsf{X}},\widetilde{\mathsf{f}})
is irreducible and non-lattice.

(2)

(\widetilde{\mathsf{X}},\widetilde{\mathsf{f}})
is stably hereditary
(see Examples
3.47
and
3.48
in §
9.3
).

(3)

\widetilde{V}_{N}:={\rm Var}(\widetilde{S}_{N})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}\infty
(because
\widetilde{V}_{N}\asymp\sum_{n=3}^{N}u_{n}^{2}\asymp V_{N}\to\infty
).

Furthermore, by the choice of
\xi_{N}
,

\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{S}_{N})\equiv\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\xi_{N}}(S_{N})=z_{N}+O(1)
, so

\frac{z_{N}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{S}_{N})}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}=O\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{V_{N}}}\bigg)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0.

Therefore
\widetilde{S}_{N}
satisfies the local limit theorem (Theorem
12.1
):

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}(\widetilde{S}_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))\sim{|a-b|}\bigg/{\sqrt{2\pi\widetilde{V}_{N}^{\xi_{N}}}}

for every
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
and
(a,b)\neq\varnothing
.

We will translate this into an asymptotic for
{\mathbb{P}}(S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b))
.
For all
N
large enough, for every
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{1}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{x}[S_{N}-z_{N}\in(a,b)]=e^{P_{N}(\xi_{N})-\xi_{N}z_{N}}\times

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(e^{\xi_{N}S_{N}}\frac{h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}(X_{N+1}^{(N)})}{e^{P_{N}(\xi_{N})}h_{1}^{\xi_{N}}(x)}\cdot\frac{h_{1}^{\xi_{N}}(x)}{h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}(X_{N+1}^{(N)})}\cdot e^{\xi_{N}(z_{N}-S_{N})}1_{(a,b)}(S_{N}-z_{N})\right)

\displaystyle=e^{P_{N}(\xi_{N})-\xi_{N}z_{N}}h_{1}^{\xi_{N}}(x)\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{x}\left(h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}(\widetilde{X}_{N+1}^{(N)})^{-1}\phi_{a,b}(\widetilde{S}_{N}-z_{N})\right)

(20.32)

where
\phi_{a,b}(t):=1_{(a,b)}(t)e^{-\xi_{N}t}
.

The pre-factor simplifies as follows. By construction
\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})}{V_{N}}=\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}.
Thus

\xi_{N}\,z_{N}-P_{N}(\xi_{N})=V_{N}\left(\xi_{N}\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}-\frac{P_{N}(\xi_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)=V_{N}\left(\xi_{N}\frac{P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})}{V_{N}}-\frac{P_{N}(\xi_{N})}{V_{N}}\right).

So

e^{P_{N}(\xi_{N})-\xi_{N}z_{N}}=e^{-V_{N}H_{N}\left(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}}\right)},

(20.33)

where
H_{N}(\eta)
is the Legendre transform of
P_{N}(\xi)/V_{N}
.

Using the mixing LLT for Markov arrays Theorem
12.3
, one can see that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}(\widetilde{X}_{N+1}^{(N)})^{-1}\phi_{a,b}(\widetilde{S}_{N}-z_{N})\right)\sim\frac{\mu_{N+1}\left({1}/{h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi\widetilde{V}_{N}^{\xi_{N}}}}\int_{a}^{b}e^{-\xi_{N}t}dt,

(20.34)

as
N\to\infty
. To do this approximate
\phi_{a,b}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
from below and above continuous functions with compact support, and approximate
h_{N+1}^{\xi_{N}}
in
L^{1}(\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{N+1},\mathfs B(\mathfrak{S}^{(N)}_{N+1}),\mu_{N+1}^{(N)})
from above and below by finite linear combinations of indicators of sets with uniformly bounded measure (here
\mu^{(N)}_{N+1}
is the distribution of
X^{(N)}_{N+1}
).

Since
\xi_{N}
is bounded, Lemma
6.86
(4) tells us that
\widetilde{V}_{N}^{\xi_{N}}\sim P_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{N})
as
N\to\infty
. Since
H_{N}(\eta)
is the Legendre transform of
P_{N}(\xi)/V_{N}
, and
P_{N}^{\prime}(\xi_{N})/V_{N}=z_{N}/V_{N}
,

\widetilde{V}^{\xi_{N}}_{N}\sim V_{N}\cdot\left(\frac{P_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{N})}{V_{N}}\right)=\frac{V_{N}}{H_{N}^{\prime\prime}(\frac{z_{N}}{V_{N}})}\hskip 11.74988pt\text{as}\hskip 11.74988ptN\to\infty.

(20.35)

Substituting (
20.33
), (
20.34
), and (
20.35
) in (
20.7
